(a) **directly reverses D179**, which put the whole rounding remainder in the drawn size precisely so the quiet zone would stop absorbing it — the owner has now seen both behaviours and prefers the size exact. The mechanism they describe is available and D179 did not consider it: only the *symbol* needs whole modules, so `size = modules·scale + 2·margin_px` is exact at every requested size with the margin carrying fractional modules. Measured: a 29-module code at 500px takes scale 14, a 406px symbol and a 47px margin — 3.36 modules, total exactly 500. It does not hold at the bottom of the range: 64px on that code gives a 1.5-module zone, and a 3-module zone needs 70px | **Moderate, and it moves a decision rather than fixing a defect.** (a) is a reversal of a shipped decision the owner approved four hours earlier, so the record has to say that plainly rather than reading as a correction; it also breaks the ±25% band at small sizes, which forces `MinSize` up or the band open, and that is a choice. (b) is view-layer, but a slider with snap points and a click-to-edit number inside `style-src 'self'` and with no new script is not free | **Approved as work 2026-08-12**, owner-chosen, into [M49](phase-details/m49.md)'s second reopening. Mechanism, band and slider stops all owner-answered in the same sitting; `MaxSize` rises 2000 → 2048 at the owner's instruction so the top stop is a round power of two | **Closed by [M49](phase-details/m49.md)'s second reopening, 2026-08-12.** (a) `qr.FitSize` returns the requested size unchanged at every value in the range: the geometry is carried as a new `qr.Style.size` in pixels with the symbol centred inside it, so only the symbol needs whole modules and the quiet zone takes the remainder. `SizeFit.Snapped`, the `want`/`got` pair in the redirect and the flash message that spent them are gone, as is the tab's paragraph explaining the snap. The ±25% band holds inside 3 to 5 modules above `(modules+10)(modules+6)/4` pixels and errs **wide** below it, only where the next scale up would break the floor — 3789 fits in band against 1083 forced, none under three, over every module count the product encodes. The low end of the range went to a floor **per code** (`qr.MinSizeFor`, 70 at 29 modules) rather than a higher `MinSize`, refused rather than clamped. Three modules is below ISO/IEC 18004's four and is measured: `make verify-scan` over a corpus grown to 1360 pictures reads 9256 of 9256 exact through two decoders at five distances. M49's first reopening's *majority of the picture is code at every size* is given up on the record — it is not simultaneously satisfiable with an exact size. (b) The control is a range input with a `<datalist>` of the owner's eight stops and an editable number beside it; no new script, `script-src 'self'` and `style-src 'self'` untouched, the two resolved by a server-side rule against a `size_shown` witness. `MaxSize` 2000 → 2048 with `MaxScale`, the `docs/SECURITY.md` raster figures, D127, D142 and the openapi bounds moved with it |

The current size is not an accident — the rationale is written in the file — so growing it is a deliberate re-spend of the damage budget, not a bug fix. How much bigger is answerable by measurement: the box can grow until the worst-case codeword count crosses the budget, and a real-scan check through the kept browser tooling is the honest gate | **Moderate as a trade, low as code**: a bigger logo spends error-correction budget that currently buys print-wear tolerance — a code that scans on screen and fails on a poster is the failure the comment names. The trade is the owner's to take; the build must state the new fraction and what remains of the budget | **Approved as work 2026-08-12 into the reopening its Severity cell names**, owner-chosen over deferral; M50.6’s scan-validation directive is in its reopened contract verbatim | **M50.6, reopened, 2026-08-12.** The box is **three tenths of the symbol's width — 9% of its area**, up from a fifth and 4%, and the half-the-budget rule the row names is superseded by three quarters (D181). The row asked for the answer to be a measurement and it is one: `make verify-scan` renders 816 pictures off the shipping path — every symbol version the product reaches, four logo shapes, the smallest, default and largest stored size of each, plus the whole version range again with no logo at all at every level as a control, two equal halves of 408 — shrinks each to 8, 6, 4, 3 and 2 pixels per module, and decodes all of it through **two independent pinned decoders**, `zxing-wasm` and `jsQR`. The check is **kept**, and it is the gate: the sweep ran 1/5 through 1/3 and **1/3 fails**, at version 13, for every logo shape and at every distance, so growing the box past what reads breaks the build. The row's own suggestion — grow until the worst-case codeword count crosses the budget — is *not* what bound the answer, and that is worth saying: the budget still has headroom at three tenths, and it is a detector failure rather than a correction failure that set the limit. **What the row's severity cell warned about is real and is recorded rather than absorbed**: `zbarimg`, older and stricter than both gating decoders, missed 12 of 1496 logo'd decodes at the old fifth and **110 at three tenths**, against 5984 of 5984 for the gating pair at either fraction, concentrated at two pixels per module rather than at any one stored size. **It reads the no-logo control clean at both — 1496 of 1496, over the whole version range at every level** — which is what makes those misses the logo's doing and not the engine's limit. Two modern engines read the larger box that far out and one older one does not — the trade, stated. |

All at `acea3fb` | Owner-reported 2026-08-12; 813² = 660,969 > 262,144 while 813 < 1024, so the total cap bit and the message could not say so. Downscale-to-fit means **decoding an image the current header caps exist to refuse**, so the decode bound has to move and be re-stated — SECURITY.md's numbered allocation story is the contract that changes | **Moderate by contract, small by code.** The caps are load-bearing numbers in SECURITY.md (decode allocation ≤ 1,048,576 bytes); accepting larger headers raises that figure and the new number must be stated, bounded by the existing 1 MiB body cap. The thumbnail-invokes-popup assertions retired with the popup; `TestTheThumbnailOpensTheQRTab` replaced them, both halves sabotaged red first, and the kept link-tabs spec drives the click from the Analytics tab. | F210 | — *(owner-reported, post-M47.5)* | **Two horizontal grey bands render across the whole world map** — one at ~60°N, one near the equator — on the analytics tab's choropleth | `internal/ui/geo/mapgen/main.go` (ring emission) and `internal/ui/geo/countries_gen.go`; observed on the running test instance at 1920×1080, chromium 1.62.1, at `a37f2d0` | Reproduced and cause isolated 2026-08-11: querying the rendered SVG for paths wider than 900 of the 1000-unit viewBox returns exactly two — **Fiji (width 1000, y≈278) and Russia (width 1000.00, y≈8)**. Both countries cross the antimeridian; mapgen emits their rings unsplit, so the path wraps 180→-180 and the fill sweeps the full map width as a band at that latitude range. Faint at 1280px, obvious at 1920 — which is why it survived every 1280-wide look since M37 shipped the map | **Moderate**: the map is wrong on every instance for every reader whose window is wide enough, and a Russia with clicks would paint its band in accent. The fix is mechanical — split rings at the antimeridian in mapgen and regenerate `countries_gen.go` — and testable: no country path's bbox may span the full viewBox width | **Unreviewed.** Falsifies [M37](phase-details/m37.md)'s claim that the map draws countries; approved as work 2026-08-11 into the reopening its Finding cell names | **[M37](phase-details/m37.md), reopened**, 2026-08-11. mapgen unwraps any ring whose consecutive longitudes jump more than 180°, clips it against the 180° meridian, and emits one ring per side flush on the frame edges; rings that do not cross pass through untouched, so the regenerated `countries_gen.go` changes exactly two lines — FJ and RU. The test the fix note asked for exists at the unit that can carry it: **per ring, not per country**, because a correctly split Russia still touches both frame edges (mainland to x=1000, Chukotka from x=0) and its per-path box legitimately spans the frame — measured so on the rendered page after the fix, both elements at width 1000 with the widest *ring* at 424.2. Written first and run against the shipped file, the test failed on **three** rings, one more than this row measured: Fiji, Russia's mainland, and Wrangel Island (996.9), whose band hid inside the mainland's. Looked at rendered at 1920×1080 on the rebuilt instance: both bands gone, console clean, `make verify-ui` green. The mechanism record is [the M37 rebuild entry](decisions.md#2026-08-11--m37-rebuilt-the-antimeridian-split-and-the-ring-as-the-unit-of-the-claim) |
| F211 | — *(owner-reported, post-M47.5)* | **The Edit tab's protections badge counts enabled booleans and the owner judges that not worth a badge** — *"if the edit badge only counts enabled booleans, I think we can go without it"* | `internal/httpx/web_links.go` (`attachTabBadges`, edit case) and `partials/link_tabs.html`, at `a37f2d0` | Owner-reported 2026-08-11, reversing their own recorded answer — the badge was owner-set at the M47/M47.5 wireframe rounds over leaving Edit unbadged (m47.5.md, D-recorded). Removal makes m47.5.md's six-position claim and the Edit-count bullet false, so it is a reopening of [M47.5](phase-details/m47.5.md), not a quiet edit | **Low**: pure removal — Edit joins Danger as a bare tab, five badge positions remain, no data or behaviour changes. The recorded decision gains a superseding entry rather than an edit | **Unreviewed**; approved as work 2026-08-11 into the reopening its Finding cell names | **[M47.5](phase-details/m47.5.md), reopened**, 2026-08-11. The edit case and its protections count are removed from `attachTabBadges`, and Edit renders bare in both states — asserted with every one of its five protections on, so no boolean resurrects the count (`internal/httpx/link_tabs_test.go`, `internal/ui/link_detail_test.go`), sabotage-verified on both sides of the seam. The kept spec's badge count moved 6 → 5, and every record enumerating six positions carries a supersession note rather than a silent count change. The wireframe-round answer's supersession is [the reopening entry](decisions.md#2026-08-11--three-reopenings-from-one-look-at-the-running-product); the build record is [the M47.5-reopened entry](decisions.md#2026-08-11--m475-reopened-the-edit-badge-goes) |
| F209 | — *(owner-reported, post-M47.5)* | **The workspace switcher's opened state is the native select popup, which nothing in the product can govern — and the owner named four symptoms on sight.** (1) The popup is not right-aligned with the fused control; it anchors where the engine puts it. (2) It opens showing the empty placeholder row — a blank option that cannot be selected, the cost the M46.6 amendment recorded, now rejected on sight. (3) Selecting a workspace paints the option's text into the `w-8` closed face, partially visible left of the chevron, until the redirect lands. (4) The popup is OS-rendered and matches nothing — *"looks like a bolted on after thought"* | `internal/ui/templates/partials/nav.html` — `workspace_switch`'s `<select>` (`6deb8fa`); the closed face is `w-8` so any selected text overflows it | Owner-reported 2026-08-11 against the running product. All four share one cause: a native select's popup cannot be positioned, styled, or purged of its placeholder cross-engine (`hidden` on `<option>` is ignored by WebKit), and its closed face renders the selected option's text by design — so no fix inside the `<select>` reaches any of the four. The shell already carries the governed alternative: both header menus are the Popover API (`button popovertarget` + `popover="auto"`, D24), styled in-product and right-aligned by the recorded viewport idiom. Symptom 3 transiently falsifies M46.6's shipped claim that *the closed face is the chevron alone* | **Moderate by ownership, not by breakage**: nothing loses data and the switch works, but the control the owner just approved from wireframes misdraws in the one state the wireframes could not show — a native popup cannot be wireframed. One edit closes all four: rebuild the opened state on the header-menu popover pattern, each workspace a submit button in the existing form, no placeholder row to draw, no select text to flash. Costs: `workspace_test.go:68-71`'s disabled-placeholder assertion encodes an owner answer whose premise (a select must display something) dissolves with the select; a11y shifts from combobox to button+group, the same recorded trade (D24) the other two menus made | **Reported by the owner 2026-08-11**, which is review, and **approved as work the same day into [M46.6](phase-details/m46.6.md)'s reopening** — symptom 3 falsifies the shipped chevron-only claim, which is what reopening exists for, and the owner chose it over deferral to Phase 4. The reopened contract names the popover pattern and the retired assertion | **[M46.6](phase-details/m46.6.md), reopened**, 2026-08-11. The opened state is rebuilt on the header menus’ Popover API pattern (D24): the chevron is a `button popovertarget` invoker, the panel is `popover="auto"` styled in the control’s own border and radius idiom, and each workspace is a submit button in the existing form — no placeholder row exists to draw, nothing can flash, the switch handler and hidden `next` untouched. Right alignment is **CSS anchor positioning**: the fused container carries `anchor-name` and the panel’s right inset is `anchor(right)` behind `@supports`, probed the same day in all three pinned engines (chromium-1234, firefox-1538, webkit-2336) — all three land the panel’s right edge exactly on the control’s; engines without it fall back to the menus’ shared-edge literal. The panel’s left inset clamps at 1rem with its width resolved from the two insets, because the kept spec’s first run caught a fixed-width panel anchored to a mid-bar control overflowing the 360px viewport’s left edge. All four symptoms are asserted where they lived: `tools/agent-browser/specs/workspace-control.spec.mjs` opens the panel, measures its right edge against the control’s, refuses any blank row, and drives a switch there and back proving the invoker’s face never carries text; the template and integration suites hold the markup, the disabled-placeholder assertion retired as approved and replaced by no-blank-entry assertions. The mechanism record is the M46.6 reopening entry in [decisions.md](decisions.md) |
| F196 | M58 | **The release-notes extraction explains itself with a false premise, and the premise is which of its two exits matters.** `release.yml` says *"The newest version is always the last section in CHANGELOG.md"*. It is the **first**: Keep a Changelog orders newest first, and 0.3.0 sits above 0.2.0 above 0.1.0 | `.github/workflows/release.yml:258`, the comment above the `awk` at `:267-272`. Against the file it describes: `## [0.3.0]` at `CHANGELOG.md:32`, `## [0.2.0]` at `:848`, `## [0.1.0]` at `:2652`, and the `[Unreleased]: https://…` definitions last | True at 0.1.0, when one released section was both newest and last, and never revisited. **The program is correct and is not what this row is about**: it has two exits, the next `## [` heading and the link-reference block (F1), and which fires depends on where the section sits — the F1 exit protects the **oldest** section, and every other version exits on the next heading. The comment presents F1 as this extraction's protection, which for 0.3.0 it is not. Verified read-only 2026-08-10 by running the program from the workflow with `ver="## [0.3.0]"`: 815 lines, exactly the 0.3.0 body (`CHANGELOG.md:33-847`), no second version heading and no reference definitions | **Low, and nothing misbehaves.** The extraction is right, the 0.3.0 notes come out clean, and no release is at risk from this. What it costs is the next person who has to reason about the two exits — they will read that the last section is the newest one, conclude the F1 exit is what guards the release they are cutting, and be wrong about which line to change. Fix note: the sentence should say the newest version is the **first** released section, that the next-heading exit is what bounds it, and that F1's exit exists for the oldest | **Reviewed 2026-08-11 — routed, and it stays open until the owner applies it.** The correction is written at `ci/proposed/release.yml` with a *Proposed* row at [W42](workflow-changes.md#made), because the path is `.github/workflows/` and nothing here can push it — the token carries no `Workflows` permission and GitHub refuses the push rather than the merge. `make workflow-proposals` reports it pending. **Not closed**: a proposal is not the fix landing, and this row is what says so until it does. Comment-only, so the extraction is untouched byte for byte and was re-run against the 0.3.0 changelog — 815 lines, clean exits. | **Applied by the owner, `91eeb45`, 2026-08-11** — [W42](workflow-changes.md#made) made. The comment now says which exit guards which end: newest first, so the reference-block exit protects the *oldest* section. `ci/proposed/release.yml` is gone, `make workflow-proposals` reports nothing pending, and the awk is untouched byte for byte, as the proposal promised. |

| F206 | M47 *(reopened)* | **htmx violates the Content-Security-Policy on every page load, in every browser, and the refusal is silent outside a console.** htmx 2.0.9 ships `includeIndicatorStyles: true`, which injects a `<style>` element for `.htmx-indicator` at load; the app's `style-src 'self'` blocks it. Fix note: `<meta name="htmx-config" content='{"includeIndicatorStyles":false}'>` is CSP-safe because it is a meta tag rather than inline script; the alternative is shipping the indicator rules in `app.css` and turning the injection off, which is the same meta tag plus three lines of CSS | **Approved as work 2026-08-11** into [M46.5](phase-details/m46.5.md), having first been approved into M47. It is a row because M58 made the erasure claim specific and a specific claim with a known exception should carry the exception in writing. Fix note: a jsonb array rewrite in the same statement, or accept and state it | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D175 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, over a recommendation that carried it. The owner's ground was class rather than severity: it is the same defect as something M58 is already fixing, and a milestone that repairs one instance and files its twin is how a tracker fills with rows reading *see the one above*. Raised at M58, out of spec when filed: F177's six metadata writers were counted before this seventh was found. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-10, by the jsonb array rewrite the row offered, in the same UPDATE as the scalar key rather than a second one — the note on `batch` forbids a second, and [F186](#closed) is what forgetting that cost. The two metadata keys merge through `\|\|` over a `jsonb_object_agg` of only the arms that matched, so each key names its own predicate once and a third would be one more arm; `jsonb_object_agg` over no matching arm is NULL and `metadata \|\| '{}'` is the row unchanged. Elements are rewritten rather than dropped, ordered by `WITH ORDINALITY`, because how many principals the role moved away from is part of what the record says; a `from` that is not an array is guarded to `'[]'` rather than erroring, so a record written by hand cannot fail a batch. The test moves the principal **out to the departing account and back**, so the surviving record's scalar `"email"` names the account that stays and its actor is nobody — which means only this scrub can clean it, and a green run cannot be F177's work or M52's label scrub doing the job. Sabotage-verified by removing the arm: the address stays in the array and the tombstone never appears in it. |

The defence that exists twelve functions away is `qrCodeFrom` (`internal/link/qr.go:435`), which does apply it on read, for exactly this reason | Read from the two call sites, not driven. `qrCodeFrom` applying `ForLogo()` on read is itself the evidence that a row can disagree with the renderer — it was written to defend against a row holding a level below H while a logo is present, which happens for a row written before [M50.6](phase-details/m50.6.md), one written by hand, or a style write that raced an upload. The size control has the same exposure and no such defence. Found 2026-08-09 while building [F171](#closed)'s re-fit, which had to reason about exactly this pair of levels | **Low, and not reachable through the product's own writes today**: both `SetQRCodeLogo` and `SetQRStyleBySlug` write H into the row whenever a logo is present, so the disagreement `qrCodeFrom` defends against cannot currently be produced from a form or an endpoint. It is a row because the defence exists at one of two sites that need it, which means somebody already judged the state reachable, and because a size that silently is not the size you asked for is the class of defect [F171](#closed) just was | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D175 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, over a recommendation that carried it. The owner's ground was class rather than severity: it is the same defect as something M58 is already fixing, and a milestone that repairs one instance and files its twin is how a tracker fills with rows reading *see the one above*. Raised at M58, out of spec when filed: F171's approved fix is the level forced on upload, and this is the size control reading a stored level rather than the drawn one. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-10, by applying `ForLogo()` to the stored style before the fit, which is the defence `qrCodeFrom` has had on read since M50.6 and the reason it has it. `SetQRSizeBySlug` now reads the row through `storedQRCode` rather than `storedQRStyle`, because the level it needs to force is a fact about the row (`HasLogo`) that the style alone does not carry; the two other callers of `storedQRStyle` copy a stored blob into a new code and are deliberately left alone. **Not reachable through the product's own writes**, as the row said — so the test writes the disagreeing row by hand, which is the only way to reach a state `qrCodeFrom` was written to defend against. Sabotage-verified at the numbers: with the fit taken against the row's `L` and the picture drawn at `H`, a request for 800px served **935px** — 135px out, against a snap tolerance of one module. |

| F176 | M52 | **Two decisions are in the log and not in the table that says it holds every one of them.** `Plan.md`'s *Phase 3 decisions* table runs D108–D145 and then stops; **D146** (the eighteen blind tasks are recorded nowhere, so M51.9's bullet is struck) and **D147** (the doc-cost judgement) exist only in [decisions.md](decisions.md) | `Plan.md:1020-1025`, the table's own caption — *"D108–D114 were taken 2026-08-06, at planning; everything below them was taken as a milestone reached it and each row says so"* — against `Plan.md:1027` onward, whose last row is D145 | `grep -c "D146\|D147" Plan.md` returns **0**, 2026-08-08. **The hole is larger than this row first said**: by M54's build the same day it was seven — D146 and D147 from M51.9, and D150–D154 from M53 — and M54's worker added its own D155–D158 while leaving the earlier gap, correctly, because this row is unreviewed and filling it is scheduling. The evidence is corrected rather than the row re-filed; what it measures is the same defect, and the count moving twice inside one day is itself the argument for fixing it. Both numbers are allocated and used: the M51.9 entry in decisions.md carries them and the index row names them. **Fifteen as of 2026-08-09**, counted mechanically at [M57.9](phase-details/m57.9.md) rather than recalled — D114, D146, D147, D150–D154, D166–D172 — because M56's and M57's decisions joined the gap and M57.9 added its own row after it, the way M54's worker did and for the same reason. The caption is what makes this a defect rather than a convention — it promises the table grows with each milestone, and the two decisions from the phase's own mid-phase review are the ones missing. Found while appending D148 and D149 to the same table, which is why the gap was visible at all: writing rows after a hole is how you find the hole | **Low.** Nothing is lost — decisions.md is the append-only record and holds both in full, with the index rows to reach them. The cost is that the table a reader consults for *what was decided* is silently two short, and the failure is invisible from either file alone: decisions.md looks complete because it is, and the table looks complete because nothing in it is wrong | **Reviewed 2026-08-09**, D174 — **no owner decision was needed**. It is a documentation correction, so workflow.md's standing approval covers it and [M58](phase-details/m58.md)'s documentation pass closes it. Raised 2026-08-08, out of [M52](phase-details/m52.md)'s spec: no bullet in m52.md is made false by it, and it is a record-keeping gap in another milestone's wake rather than anything the erasure work touches. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, by the documentation pass, as D174 said it would be — the row needed no owner decision, being a correction to a record under workflow.md's standing approval. **The gap was thirteen**, counted mechanically against the file rather than taken from either number on record: this row's own evidence said fifteen and D174's recount said twelve, and both were wrong in different directions. D114 and D172 had landed in the table between the count and the fix, so two of the fifteen were already there; **D168 was in neither count**. Added: D146, D147, D150–D154 and D166–D171. Plan.md's *Phase 3 decisions* table is now **D108–D176** with no holes, in ascending order and with no duplicates, verified by script rather than by reading — 69 rows, re-derived 2026-08-10 after D175 and D176 landed in the same milestone. The number was D174 when this cell was first written, which is the same staleness one iteration smaller: a cell naming the table's last row is wrong the moment the table grows, so what it asserts is the *property* — no holes, ascending, unique — and the range is evidence for it rather than the claim. The caption that made this a defect rather than a convention — *everything below them was taken as a milestone reached it and each row says so* — is now true again. |

The two coverage rows each require ≥12 distinct countries and state their purpose as *"the choropleth as a map rather than as one shaded country"* and *"that the seeded codes are ones the 110m map has shapes for"* — so the test built to prevent an empty map passes while the map is unreachable, which is the failure mode a coverage test is supposed to be immune to. Suppression now follows the **data** and not the configuration: `fillLinkAnalytics` drives the empty state, the choropleth and the ranked list from `data.GeoAvailable` **or** `hasResolvedCountries(...)`, which excludes `""` and `"unknown"` exactly as `countryValues` does and keys on clicks-or-visitors rather than the metric on screen. Configuration still counts, so *database configured, no clicks yet* keeps its ordinary **No data yet** rather than a claim about the instance — the sentence is reached only when both are false. **Both are evaluated per link and per window, not per instance**, which is narrower than this row's own fix and the claims first written around it: `stats` is one link over the 7, 30 or 90 days on screen, so a database-less instance holding country history still shows the sentence on a link with no country inside the selected window. Shown red by reverting to the configuration check, which reported the choropleth undrawn for an instance holding country history. |
| F161 | Phase 3 planning | **The domains page can state, in one response, that a hostname is verified and that it is not.** Pressing *Check DNS* on a domain inside D70's grace window renders the flash *"&lt;host&gt; is not verified: …"* directly above a row whose badge reads *"Verified — links are served here"* | Flash: `internal/link/verification.go:199-205`, surfaced by `internal/httpx/web_domains.go:230-245` into `internal/ui/templates/pages/domains.html:17`. The defect is that the manual re-check returns a `ValidationErrors` string asserting *not verified* while the badge it renders beside re-reads `verified_at` and asserts the opposite. The grace-window pair the owner first noticed (*Verified* beside *"links … stop being served at &lt;time&gt;"*) is **not** a contradiction: during grace the hostname genuinely is served, and `StopsAt` is guarded on `VerifiedAt != nil` at `verification.go:113` so the two can only appear together. **Second limb, ≤1 hour wide:** between grace expiry and the next hourly pass, `StopsAt` is in the past and `domains.html:96-98` has no past/future branch, so the page reads *"stop being served at &lt;a past time&gt;"* beside *"links are served here"*. Minimal reproduction, live on the demo at the time of writing: `/domains` → *Check DNS* on `go.linkctrl.example`. The cost is that the one page an operator consults to find out whether their hostname works contradicts itself, on the exact action taken to resolve the doubt | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. D174 resolves that conflict in D114's favour rather than approving afresh, so the approval dates from 2026-08-07. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, **both limbs**. The flash: `MarkDomainVerificationFailed`'s returned row is now kept and the refusal branches on its `VerifiedAt`, so a serving hostname inside D70's grace window is told it *failed its DNS check* and that its links are still served until a stated time, while a never-verified one keeps the original sentence verbatim. The clause is built in **database time** — both timestamps come out of the same write, so no app clock is consulted. The past deadline: `StopsAtPassed` is computed by a pure `domainStopsAt` with `now` injected, and the template branches on it. The grace-window pair that is **not** a contradiction is byte-identical and asserted to still render. Shown red twice, once per limb, including the finding's own reproduction. |
| F162 | Phase 3 planning | **The demo is guaranteed to lose its only verified custom hostname 24 hours after every reseed, and the coverage row that exists to prevent that will fail rather than catch it.** `demoCoverage()` asserts exactly one verified custom domain, bounded above and below; the domain it counts can never re-verify against a real resolver | `cmd/lctl/demo_coverage_test.go:478-485` (`Min: 1, Max: 1`); seeder `cmd/lctl/demo_phase2.go:1434-1498` with the in-process stub resolver at `:198`, `:203-223`; the job that unverifies is `internal/link/verification.go:352-483` via `UnverifyDomain` (`internal/store/query/domains.sql:292-308`) | `lctl demo` verifies `go.linkctrl.example` through `VerifyDomain` against `demoChallengeResolver`, a map that lives inside the seeder process and dies with it. The long-running server wires the real resolver (`cmd/linkctrl/main.go:615` → `internal/dnsx/dnsx.go:39-47`), and `go.linkctrl.example` is an RFC 2606 `.example` name that cannot resolve for anybody. The hourly `domains` job (`cmd/linkctrl/jobs.go:295-301`) has therefore failed the check every hour since seeding — 13 consecutive passes visible in the demo app log — and at `verification_failing_since + DOMAIN_VERIFY_GRACE` it calls `UnverifyDomain` and the count becomes **0**. Read from the demo database 2026-08-07 01:04 UTC: `go.linkctrl.example` has `verified_at` set, `verification_failing_since = 2026-08-06 11:31:08+00`, grace 24h unset therefore default, so it unverifies at **2026-08-07 11:31:08+00**. The assertion's own comment says the ceiling *"is the whole assertion"* — one hostname verified, one not — and after that moment the demo shows neither state. **The coverage row cannot catch this, and the reason is structural rather than a bug in the row:** `newDemoDB` (`cmd/lctl/demo_coverage_test.go:1133-1147`) creates a throwaway database per run, seeds into it and asserts in the same instant, so it never reads the live demo and no hourly job ever runs against what it measured. The assertion is true at seed time and stays true forever in the only database it looks at. `make test-integration` and CI are therefore **unaffected** — this breaks nothing that is checked, which is the whole difficulty | **Moderate.** The demo silently stops demonstrating the feature M40 built, an hour at a time, and the mechanism built to guarantee otherwise is incapable of observing it: it measures a database that is destroyed before the state can arise. Nothing fails, nothing goes red, and the only signal is somebody opening the page. Related to F160: both are `demoCoverage()` rows asserting a state the running demo cannot sustain, and this one adds the reason — **the coverage test measures the seed, not the instance** | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. The column was stale rather than open: [D114](decisions.md) approved all seven at planning — *"a defect does not need a milestone: it is a row in deferred-findings.md and it is fixed at M58 alongside the rest"* — and this row never recorded it. D174 resolves that conflict in D114's favour rather than approving afresh, so the approval dates from 2026-08-07. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09 — **by a setting, not by code**. `scripts/instance.sh` writes `LINKCTRL_DOMAIN_VERIFY_INTERVAL=0` into a freshly generated `.env.demo`, which disables the pass and the host reload and leaves verification on-demand only, exactly as the variable is documented to mean. The two alternatives are recorded at the statement and both are worse: an unbounded grace leaves the domains page reading *verification failing since …* for the life of the instance, which is the product telling the truth and the demo showing a broken feature; a hostname a real resolver can satisfy needs a TXT record in a zone somebody owns, which no seeder can publish. `TestTheDemoInstanceDoesNotReverifyWhatTheSeederStubbed` asserts the pair — the seeder verifies only RFC 2606 `.example` names, **and** the generator sets the interval to 0 — and reads the generator rather than `.env.demo`, which is untracked. **The running demo needs the line added by hand**: `instance.sh init` refuses to touch an env file that exists, and a worker may not touch the demo instance. |

The costs are an audit entry recording a grant that changed nothing, a duplicate row a reader must reconcile, and a confirmation that is false as written | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. The column was stale rather than open: [D114](decisions.md) approved all seven at planning — *"a defect does not need a milestone: it is a row in deferred-findings.md and it is fixed at M58 alongside the rest"* — and this row never recorded it. D174 resolves that conflict in D114's favour rather than approving afresh, so the approval dates from 2026-08-07. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, and the grant is **not** refused — D31 stands. Two fixes, both to what is said. `MemberGrant` keeps the returned member, asks whether an organization-wide role of rank ≤ the granted one already exists, and redirects to a notice that says the grant changed nothing; on a lookup error it falls back to the weaker claim rather than the false one. The warning is now a `#grant-note` conditioned on the selected target, workspace and role, with three outcomes — refused, changes-nothing, widens — refreshed by `hx-get`/`hx-select` on all three selects and **correct server-rendered without script**. Subsumption is by rank, and the assumption that the four built-ins nest is written into the doc comment with `00700_seed.sql` named as the only `INSERT INTO roles` in the tree. The API needed nothing: `GrantWorkspaceAccess` returns 201 with the membership and asserts no effect. Shown red at the equal-rank boundary and on the unconditional redirect. |
| F164 | Phase 3 planning | **Every bar chart in this product labels its axis ceiling as the peak.** The dashboard reads *"peak 5,000/day"* on a series whose true maximum is 2,351 — a 113% overstatement, and the only number the chart carries | `internal/ui/funcs.go:93` assigns `c.MaxY = top`, where `top = niceCeil(maxV)` (`:71`, `:257-273`) rather than `maxV`; rendered as the peak at `internal/ui/templates/partials/series_chart.html:18`. The defect is that the rounded ceiling is then presented as an observation. The overstatement is unbounded within a decade — a true peak of 2,001 renders as 5,000, 149% high. **Two adjacent observations, recorded here rather than as their own rows because they share the file and the fix:** `Tick.Label` is populated at `funcs.go:94-97` and never rendered — `series_chart.html:7-9` emits only `<line>` elements — so the 16 units `PlotH = h - 16` reserves for labels (10% of the box) are spent on nothing; and the same rounding is what makes the plot look empty, since fill is `maxV / niceCeil(maxV)` ∈ (40%, 100%] and the demo sits at 46.5%. Nobody reading *peak 5,000/day* has any way to discover the peak was 2,351. The scaling complaint that found it is a design choice and is not claimed as a defect here | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. The column was stale rather than open: [D114](decisions.md) approved all seven at planning — *"a defect does not need a milestone: it is a row in deferred-findings.md and it is fixed at M58 alongside the rest"* — and this row never recorded it. D174 resolves that conflict in D114's favour rather than approving afresh, so the approval dates from 2026-08-07. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09. `Chart.Peak` is the largest reading; `MaxY` keeps the `niceCeil` ceiling and its doc now says it is not a reading. **The scaling algorithm is untouched** — the row is explicit that the complaint which found it is a design choice, not a defect. Both adjacent observations went with it: the computed `Tick.Label`s are now drawn, in an outer viewport with no viewBox nested around a `preserveAspectRatio="none"` plot so the digits are not stretched (at `max-w-6xl` the box is 1.49× its units wide), each on a `fill-sunken` chip because `subtle` on `fill-accent-hover` measures **1.82:1** light and 3.15:1 dark against 6.84:1 and 12.0:1 on the chip; and a third tick labelled `0` finally spends the 16 units `PlotH = h - 16` had always reserved. Only utilities already in the built `app.css`, both themes, no CSS rebuild, CSP unchanged. `TestBarChartGeometry` gained a note that it **cannot** tell ceiling from peak — its series makes the two coincide, which is how this survived to be found. |

Found while writing M47's tests: the click-limit assertion needed `Link.ClicksConsumed` in the fixture for the same reason, which is what surfaced the pattern | **Low, and entirely in the tests.** Nothing ships wrongly and no claim on any page is false; a browser renders all six correctly. The fixture now passes a `linkForm` **struct** mirroring `httpx.linkFormData` field for field, which fails at compile time where the map failed silently. It cannot be the production type — httpx imports ui, and `ui` is stdlib-only — so a mirror is the closest available and the mirroring is the thing the test asserts. | F169 | M47 | **The demo's second workspace has never held a single link, so the one thing it exists to demonstrate — that the list, the tags and the analytics change when you switch — is not demonstrated.** `seedSecondWorkspace` cannot move the owner into the workspace it just created, and every link it makes lands in the first one instead. The mechanism is one row: `select default_workspace_id, last_workspace_id from users where email='dev@killerofpie.com'` returns **both** pointing at Default. `actAs` sets rung 3 and the demo owner has rung 2 pinned, so rung 2 wins and the identity `actAs` returns is still in Default — `link.Service.Create` then reads `actor.WorkspaceID` (`internal/link/service.go:328`) and files the link there. The seeder reports success either way, because nothing compares where a link was asked for with where it went. `demoWorkspace2Catalogue()`'s own comment says its job is to *"make workspace scoping observable"*; it is not observable, and has not been | **Moderate.** No product defect — this is the demo seeder, and `ResolveWorkspaceForUser`'s precedence is correct and deliberate. It is also the upstream half of [F168](#closed) — those five aliases sitting in the wrong workspace are what jammed `make demo-update` — so the reset now recovers from a state that should not arise. **Nothing catches it**: the inherited *a new feature somebody can see* rule points at `demoCoverage()`, and no row there constrains which workspace anything is in | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. Raised 2026-08-07 while fixing F168, out of that fix's spec, and it blocked nothing: `make demo-update` succeeds either way. Approved with the rest of the demo cluster — F160, F162, F165, F166 — because the demo is the first thing an evaluator opens. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09. `actAs` clears the pinned default (rung 2) through the product's own `SetDefaultWorkspaceForUser` before writing last-used (rung 3), then **re-resolves and checks** that the identity landed where it was asked to, erroring if not — so the seeder can no longer report success while filing links somewhere else. Clearing rather than re-pinning is deliberate and recorded: re-pinning would leave the pin on the second workspace for the length of the seeding, and a run dying in that window leaves an account `demoActor` then refuses to seed as. | F170 | M49 | **A refused QR style posts the reader off the panel's own page and onto the link page**, so somebody working at `/links/{id}/qr` who types a bad value is answered with a different page and has to find their way back to the one they were on | `internal/httpx/web_qr.go`, `finishQRAction` — it calls `h.loadLinkDetail` and renders `"link_detail"` unconditionally, while the write's success path goes through `qrReturn`, which *does* distinguish the two surfaces by matching the form's `next` field | Read from the two paths, not driven in a browser. The form carries `next` precisely so a save can land back where the reader is standing (M48, `QRReturn`), and the refusal path ignores it. Predates M49 — M48 wrote both — but M49 makes it reachable in a new way: the size box is the panel's only free-text field, and an out-of-range size is now the ordinary refusal rather than a value the old number inputs' `min`/`max` mostly prevented. The refusal itself is correct: 422, the message beside the form, nothing stored | **Minor.** Cosmetic and recoverable — the reader sees the same form, with the same message, on the link page instead of the panel page, and every control still works. Raised 2026-08-07, out of M49's spec: no bullet in m49.md is made false by it, the refusal is correct and the milestone's claims about size, snapping and the PNG all hold on either page. Approved because `next` already exists and is ignored on one path only. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09. `finishQRAction` takes `next` and `slug`, and when `next` equals the path it builds itself it assembles the same data `LinkQRPage` does and renders `"link_qr"` at 422 — **matched, never followed**, on `qrReturn`'s terms. The refusal is otherwise unchanged: 422, the message beside the form, nothing stored. Shown red on both surfaces and both directions. |
| F171 | M50.6 | **Uploading a logo can make a code's PNG download stop working.** A logo forces error correction to level H, which grows the symbol; the stored margin and scale are carried forward unchanged, so a code already near the 2000px raster bound is pushed past it and `GET …/qr.png` starts answering `422` for a code that downloaded a moment earlier | `internal/link/qr.go`, `SetQRCodeLogo` — it rewrites the style's level and nothing else; the refusal is `RenderQRPNGBySlug`'s `qr.ErrTooLarge` arm, and the growth is `qr.OutputSize` over a larger `Code.Size` | Measured 2026-08-07 in `internal/qr`: an 89-byte payload (`https://links.example/` + a 60-character alias + `?src=qr`) encodes to **37 modules at level L** and **53 at H**. `qr.FitSize(37, 2000)` returned margin 13, scale 31 — then a legitimate style the size control would produce — drawing **1953px**. *(Amended 2026-08-12 at M49's reopening and again 2026-08-13 at its second, which reversed the first: D182 makes the requested size exact, so `FitSize(37, 2000)` returns scale 44 with a 186-pixel quiet zone — 4.2 modules — and draws **2000px**, the number asked for. D179's intermediate answer was margin 4, scale 44 and 1980px. Either way the size control cannot produce margin 13 at any request. That shape is now reachable only over the API or from a row written before this date, which is what the test that reproduces this finding hand-builds. The finding's own arithmetic is untouched — 89 bytes, 37 modules at L, 53 at H — because it is a property of the payload and the level, not of the fit.)* Uploading a logo raises the level, and the same margin and scale on the 53-module symbol draw **2449px**, past `qr.MaxSize`. The SVG is unaffected (no raster bound) and the dashboard's inline drawing is unaffected; what breaks is the PNG download and the PNG the panel links to. The existing refusal is correct and its message already says what to do — *set a smaller size and download it again* — so the reader is not stranded, but nothing warned them and the size they set is no longer the size they get | **Minor.** Recoverable in one action, with a message that names it, and reachable only from a style already within about 20% of the raster ceiling. It is a row rather than nothing because the cause is invisible from the surface: the reader uploaded an image and a download stopped working. The fix is a decision rather than a patch — re-fit the size at H on upload (which restyles a code that may be printed) or refuse the upload for a code that would outgrow the bound (which refuses a picture the SVG can still draw) — which is why it is not taken here | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174, **and the design question the row raised is answered with it** — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. The owner chose **re-fit the size at H on upload**, silently, over refusing the upload: the payload is unchanged so a code already printed still scans, and refusing would say no to a picture the SVG draws correctly. Raised 2026-08-07, out of M50.6's spec: no bullet in m50.6.md is made false by it. The occlusion cap, the level forcing, the two-output parity and the contract all hold; the picture is drawn correctly at every size the raster path accepts. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, taking D174's answer exactly: **re-fit at level H on upload, silently.** `refitForLogo` encodes at both levels, takes the pre-upload output size as the target — clamped into `[MinSize, MaxSize]`, because a pre-M49 style can describe a picture outside the range — and applies `qr.FitSize`'s margin and scale. Any failure returns `ForLogo()` alone, so a fit that cannot be made never costs a logo already written. **No user-facing copy was added**, which is the part the owner took less than the recommendation on. The row's own arithmetic was reproduced and then closed: 37 modules at L, 53 at H, 1953px before, 2449px after, and **1953px exactly** with the re-fit. *(Amended 2026-08-12 at M49's reopening and again 2026-08-13 at its second, without changing what M58 did: `refitForLogo` still targets the pre-upload size, and since D182 a fit lands on that number exactly — `FitSize(53, 1953)` returns scale 32 with a 128-pixel quiet zone — so the same case comes back to **1953px**, where it started. D179's intermediate snap to a 61-module span put it one pixel off, at 1952px. The 1953px and 2449px halves are unchanged; both are `OutputSize` over a hand-built style, which no fit is consulted for.)* One shipped assertion had to be amended rather than kept — `TestTheDefaultCodeCarriesALogoToo` asserted an upload changes the level *and nothing else about the picture*, which D174 makes false on purpose. |
| F175 | M51.9 | **The mailer-free `/forgot` page promises the mail it is about to refuse.** Its subtitle reads *"We send a link to your address"* directly above a card headed *"This instance cannot send mail"* | `internal/ui/templates/pages/forgot.html:5` (the subtitle) against `:15` (the refusal heading) — the header block renders unconditionally, above the no-mailer branch | Seen in a browser on a mailer-free instance, 2026-08-08: the rendered page is, in order, *"Reset your password"* / *"We send a link to your address"* / *"This instance cannot send mail"* / *"…no mail relay is configured here, so there is nothing to send it with."* [M51](phase-details/m51.md)'s bullet is met — the form does say the instance has no mailer and does name the operator's route — so this is the sentence above it rather than the refusal itself. Same shape as [F161](#closed): one response asserting a capability and its absence | **Low.** A reader gets the right answer two lines later. It is on the page an operator reaches precisely when something is not working, which is where a contradiction costs most | **Approved as work 2026-08-08**, M51.9's triage — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. One line of template, and the cheapest of the four to carry | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09. One line: the `/forgot` subtitle is `{{if not .NoMailer}}`-guarded, so a mailer-free instance renders *Reset your password* straight into *This instance cannot send mail* and every other instance is unchanged. Shown red by removing the guard. |
| F177 | M52 | **Erasure scrubs the actor snapshot and not an address carried in an audit record's `metadata`.** An erased account's address survives in the detail of records where the account is the *subject* of somebody else's action — and in one case in the detail of its own record | `internal/store/query/accounts.sql`, `EraseDeletedAccounts` — the three label columns and nothing else. The writers: `internal/invite/invite.go:437` (`invitation.created`) and `:860` (`invitation.redeemed`), `internal/team/member.go:197`, `:258`, `:386` (`member.added`, `member.removed`, `member.role_changed`), `internal/instance/instance.go:502` | Six sites, counted rather than recalled, every one of them writing the key `"email"`. Reproduced by the demo seeder: the departed account joins by redeeming an invitation, and after `seedDeletedAccount` has deleted and erased it, its `invitation.redeemed` record reads `actor_label = 'deleted account'` with `metadata->>'email'` still holding the address. The administrator's `invitation.created` record holds it too, and that one has a *live* actor — so the row that carries an erased person's address is somebody else's audit entry. Not reachable without `audit.read`, which is not delegable to an API key, and the records are governed by `AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS` | **Moderate.** No exposure beyond an existing permission and no loss, but it is a limit on a claim the product now makes about itself, which is the kind of gap a compliance reader is entitled to have stated rather than to discover. It is stated: `docs/SECURITY.md`'s *What is not defended* names it, and Plan.md's discharged row points here | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, and **bundled with F181**: both are an erased address surviving somewhere the erasure pass does not reach, and that pass is opened for F181 anyway. Raised at M52, out of its spec and deliberately not decided inside it — m52.md identifies the residue as the actor snapshot, with a file:line, and D148 settles only what the tombstone is. Both halves the owner weighed stand unchanged: the address sits inside a record of what somebody *else* did, so removing it edits their audit entry, and the only mechanism that reaches it — matching `metadata->>'email'` against the erased address — is a sequential scan of a partitioned table that is the largest thing in this schema after analytics. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, **bundled with [F181](#closed)** as D174 directed — one erasure pass, opened once. `EraseDeletedAccounts` now scrubs the `"email"` key inside `audit_logs.metadata`, case-folded on both sides because `invitation.created` stores what the administrator typed. **Seven writers, not the six this row counted**: the row's `instance.go:502` is stale and `instance.go` writes two. Written by the **same** `UPDATE` as `actor_label` rather than a second one, and that is not tidiness — Postgres applies one of two data-modifying CTEs that write the same row and silently drops the other, which the existing tombstone test caught immediately, and an `invitation.redeemed` record is both the erased actor's and carries their address. The new `batch` CTE aggregates rather than joining `pending`, for the same hazard across a batch of more than one. **`HAVING count(*) > 0` is load-bearing** and was proved with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE)`: with it an idle pass shows `never executed` on all seven partitions; without it every partition is scanned hourly on an instance where nobody has ever deleted an account. Shown red in both directions, including the over-reach one. |
| F178 | M54 | **An owner cannot see that an organization was cut out of their account-wide key's reach.** The key list reports one reach — pinned or account-wide — and nothing else, so a credential that has silently stopped resolving into one tenant reads on the page exactly as it did the day before | `internal/store/query/apikeys.sql`, `ListAPIKeysForUser` selects `organization_id` and no revocation rows; `api_key_org_revocations` (`internal/store/migrations/04200_apikey_account_reach.sql`) is read only by `ResolveOrganizationForAPIKey` and `GetAPIKeyByPrefix`, both on the authentication path; `internal/auth/apikey.go` `APIKeyInfo` has no field for it | Read from the query and the struct, not driven in a browser. `TestAnAdministratorCutsTheirOrganizationOutOfAnAccountWideKey` writes the row and asserts the key stops resolving into that organization, so the state is real and reachable in one administrative action. The owner's evidence is a credential that works everywhere it used to except one tenant, with no page saying why — and the audit record that *does* say why is written in the administrator's organization, which the owner may hold no `audit.read` in. The administrator's own view is not affected: they took the action and it is in their log | **Minor**, and it is a gap rather than a defect — nothing is wrong, something is unsaid. The cost is a support question that cannot be answered from the product: *why does my key work in Acme and not in Beta*. It grows with the number of organizations an account belongs to, which is the same number that makes account-wide keys worth having. Fix note: one query keyed on the listed ids and one repeated field on the key representation, which is also an OpenAPI change and a column on two surfaces | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, over a recommendation to carry it. The owner took it as **F183's other half**, so M54's reach revocation closes on the seeing side as well as the acting side rather than staying half-told. Raised 2026-08-08, out of M54's spec: m54.md requires the reach revoke be *distinguishable from a full revoke*, which the separate audit action and the surviving row satisfy; it asks nothing about the owner's view, and building one would have added a query, a response field, a schema and two columns nothing in the milestone called for — which is what it now costs. | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, taken **against** the recommendation to carry, as F183's other half. A new `ListAPIKeyOrgRevocations` keyed on the listed ids and joined to `organizations`, one extra round trip in `List` and skipped when there are no keys; `RevokedOrganizations` on the key representation, `[]` rather than `null`; the API schema on all three key responses, all of which are `additionalProperties: false`. `revoked_by` is deliberately not exposed. The dashboard renders it as a sub-line under **Reach** rather than a new column, because a column would be empty on every row of every instance that has never done this, and what the bars qualify *is* the reach. One test-quality note kept: the first page assertion was a bare `strings.Contains` on the organization name and **passed against a template rendering the bare uuid**, because the fixture's organization shared a name with the chrome — which is precisely why the sabotage step exists. |

A ten-line test comparing anchor order to heading order would, and would have caught F143 too | **Reviewed 2026-08-09**, D174, and the row splits. The **reorder** is a documentation correction under workflow.md's standing approval and needs no owner decision; [M58](phase-details/m58.md)'s documentation pass makes it. | F180 | M57 | **Two leaders of the domains family record one custom-domain verification twice.** The pass decides whether to write a `domain.verified` audit record from `full.VerifiedAt`, which it read *before* its own write, rather than from what the write returned — so two passes that both read the row unverified both record the event, and the idempotent `UPDATE` underneath them hides nothing because it was never the thing being duplicated | `internal/link/verification.go:400-406` — the `if full.VerifiedAt == nil` branch and the `recordDomainEvent` inside it, against the `full` read at `:371`. `MarkDomainVerified` itself is safe (`internal/store/query/domains.sql:230-231`, *"sets verified_at only when it is not already set"*), which is why the duplicate is a record and not a state | **Reproduced, five runs of five.** Two concurrent `ReverifyDomains` passes on one pool against a freshly registered hostname whose challenge resolves produced **2 `domain.verified` audit records** for one verification, every time; the probe was written against `newDomains` in `test/integration/custom_domains_test.go`, run, and deleted. The *failing* path did not duplicate in the same probe — 1 `domain_failing` notification of 1 — because the second pass usually re-reads `verification_failing_since` after the first has COALESCEd it, so that half is a narrower race and is not claimed here. `UnverifyDomain` is genuinely closed: its `verified_at IS NOT NULL` predicate makes the second leader's write affect zero rows and the audit record is never reached. **The one other read-then-write guard in the eight families was probed and did not reproduce**: `notify.Service.WarnAuditGrowth` (`internal/notify/notify.go:797-805`) reads `NotifiedSince` and then inserts, which is the same shape, but two concurrent calls produced **1 notification, five runs of five** — the two statements have nothing slow between them, so the second caller sees the first's row. It is recorded here rather than filed, because a race nobody has shown is a report and not a finding; if it is ever shown, it is this row's shape at a second site and needs a different fix, since `Notify` is an unconditional insert with nothing to branch on | **Minor.** Nothing is lost, nothing is served wrongly, and no state diverges — the duplicate is one extra row in an audit log, on a hostname that was correctly verified. What it costs is that the audit trail stops being a count of events: somebody reconciling *how many times was this hostname verified* gets two for one. It is also the **only** one of the eight job families whose second-layer defence is a read rather than a write, which is worth more than the row's own severity — the pattern is what to watch for, and the domains pass is the most exposed to the window because it is the longest (a batch of DNS lookups, bounded at ten minutes) | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, D174 — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**. Raised at [M57](phase-details/m57.md) while enumerating what each of the eight families does under two leaders, which is that milestone's own bullet — and m57.md says in as many words that the unsafe one *"is a finding rather than something this milestone quietly fixes"*, so it was filed rather than repaired. The fix was stated in the row so the review was about scheduling rather than design: branch on `verified.VerifiedAt` (what `MarkDomainVerified` returned) instead of on `full.VerifiedAt` (what was read a DNS round trip earlier). | **[M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, 2026-08-09, by the fix this row stated: the pass branches on what `MarkDomainVerified` returned rather than on what it read a DNS round trip earlier. `newlyVerified` exploits what the write already guarantees — `verified_at = COALESCE(verified_at, now())` beside `updated_at = now()` in one statement means the two are equal exactly when this call filled the NULL — so no SQL changed and no column was added. The residual is documented rather than claimed away: two transactions sharing a microsecond `now()` remain possible, because `now()` is taken before the second writer blocks on the row lock. The integration test makes the interleaving **deterministic** through the file's existing `stubZone.interrupt` rather than probabilistic, and reproduced the finding's 2-of-1 exactly when reverted. **The manual *Verify* button has the same defect twelve lines away and is [F185](#closed)**, filed rather than folded in, because this row scopes itself to the job family. |

The scrub that does not reach it: `internal/store/query/accounts.sql:149-231` (`EraseDeletedAccounts` — three label columns and the account row, and nothing else). **There is no purge**: `invitations.sql` declares `RevokeLapsedInvitation` and no delete, and no job removes the row | Reproduced end to end on the test instance 2026-08-09, through the product rather than by SQL: an account invited into an organization, redeemed, then deleted through `POST /account/delete` and erased by the hourly pass. An **outstanding** invitation addressed to the same text is deliberately left alone and the test proves that direction too: it is an offer to an address rather than a record of a person, the address became reusable the moment the account was deleted, and blanking it would break the redemption comparison for whoever takes it next. Empty string rather than the tombstone, for the reason `users.email` is one — redemption compares against this column, and a placeholder that reads like a label is a value that comparison would then have to rule out. `invitations_outstanding_email_key` cannot collide on the blanks: it excludes redeemed rows, which is every row this reaches. |

The service is not what is wrong: `Invites.Roles` and the D43 cap both bound the list to the actor's own rank, which is why the default is the actor's *maximum* rather than the instance's | **Low to moderate**, and it is a default rather than a hole. The tests scope to each `<select>` by id, because a member row whose role is editor emits identical option markup and a whole-body match would pass on the wrong control. Shown red by flipping the rank comparison, by removing each `selected` limb in turn, and by blanking `Form.Role` in both loaders. |
| F183 | M57.9 | **Cutting an organization out of an account-wide key's reach stops it acting there and not reading about it.** `GET /api/v1/workspaces` goes on listing the barred organization's name, slug and workspace ids to the revoked key, because the bound it applies is *pinned or not* rather than *reachable or not* | `internal/auth/workspace.go:131` — `if actor.IsAPIKey() && actor.APIKeyOrgID != nil && r.OrganizationID != *actor.APIKeyOrgID`, and `APIKeyOrgID` is nil for exactly the keys a reach revocation applies to. | F184 | M57.9 | **`/account/mfa` scrolls sideways on a phone**, by 174px at a 360px viewport — the one page in the dashboard that does. The outermost offending element is a single `<svg>`, 488px wide, right edge at 534 against a 360px viewport; `/account` on the same sweep is 0. It is enrolment-state dependent — the QR is only drawn while the second factor is off, which is the state somebody setting one up is in, and [m53.md](phase-details/m53.md):56 names enrolling *"from the same device"* as a case it designed for | **Low.** Nothing is unreachable: the page scrolls, the secret is printed in text beside the QR for exactly this reader, and every control still works. It is worth a row because it is the first thing to break the redesign's headline property since M46 shipped it, and because the amendment that narrowed M46's bullet at its reopening named *"a fixed-width SVG"* as what the scan would not catch — two milestones before M53 added one. Fix note: `max-w-full h-auto` on the emitted SVG, or a `max-w-full` wrapper, either of which the scan still cannot assert | **Approved as work 2026-08-09**, M57.9's triage — **carried to [M58](phase-details/m58.md)**, and the owner asked for **both halves**: constrain the SVG *and* make the test able to see it, which the recommended option had left out. The SVG: `qr.FluidClass` (`max-w-full h-auto`) on the emitted root for the inline renders, both utilities already in the shipped `app.css` so no stylesheet rebuild is involved — an inline `style` was not available, the dashboard CSP being `style-src 'self'` with no `unsafe-inline`. **The `.svg` download is byte-identical**, because a class list resolves only against the dashboard's stylesheet and that document leaves the product. `mfa.html` needed no change: `max-w-full` on the child resolves against the `w-40 … p-1` box, where a `max-w-full` **wrapper** would not have constrained a fixed-width child at all. The test: `overflow_test.go` now also checks `<svg>` with a stated pixel width past the 360px viewport, accepting either a scrolling ancestor or `max-w-full` on the element — a QR code inside a scroll box is one you must drag into view before you can photograph it — and the header's *what it does not enforce* list no longer names a fixed-width SVG. **The fixture is why the scan was blind**: `mfa`'s `QR` stub said `width="8"`, and it now carries what `qr.Render` actually emits. Re-measured in Chromium at 360×780 against the real page over a real stylesheet: **142 → 0**, the 32px difference from the row's 174 being the fixture's 456px symbol against the real account's 488px. |
| F173 | M51.9 | **A configured but unreachable SMTP relay blocks the whole server from starting, for the full SMTP timeout, at every boot.** The reachability probe runs before the HTTP listener does, so nothing — including the redirect path — is served until the relay answers or the dial times out | `cmd/linkctrl/main.go:395`, `sender.Verify(ctx)` called synchronously in `run`; the listener does not start until `internal/platform/httpserver/server.go:76` is reached, later in the same function; the timeout is `LINKCTRL_SMTP_TIMEOUT` (`internal/config/config.go`, `docs/configuration.md:600`, default `10s`) | **Measured on the test instance, 2026-08-08**, with `LINKCTRL_SMTP_HOST=192.0.2.1` (RFC 5737, routes nowhere): `starting` at 06:52:01.216, relay error at 06:52:11.262, `http server listening` at 06:52:11.266 — **10.05s of dead server**, equal to the timeout. At `LINKCTRL_SMTP_TIMEOUT=300s` the container never became healthy at all and `docker compose up --wait` gave up with *"container linkctrl-test-app-1 is unhealthy"*. The code's own comment at `main.go:386-394` is what this contradicts: *"A warning and not a fatal error, for the same reason Redis is: the relay being down is not a reason for a link shortener to stop serving redirects."* It is not fatal and it does stop serving. Redis is not comparable — its dial timeout is `1s` (`config.go:123`) against SMTP's `10s`. Not an M46–M51 defect: this is [M26](phase-details/m26.md)'s mailer, found because [M51](phase-details/m51.md) is what makes a mailer worth configuring | **Moderate.** Nobody has met it because `SMTP_HOST` unset is the default and an unset host skips the probe entirely — M51 is the milestone that gives operators a reason to set one. It lands on [M56](phase-details/m56.md)/[M57](phase-details/m57.md): a rolling deploy's new container is unavailable for the timeout at every start, and a healthcheck with a shorter start period fails it. The fix is to run the probe after the listener, or in a goroutine | **Approved as work 2026-08-08**, M51.9's triage. **Scheduled into [M56](phase-details/m56.md)**, not M58 — it is a failover defect and M56 is the failover contract, and leaving it for the phase's last milestone would have [M57](phase-details/m57.md) measure a rolling deploy against a replica that is dead for ten seconds at every start | **[M56](phase-details/m56.md)**, the failover milestone it was scheduled into. The probe now runs in a goroutine of its own (**D166**): *after* the listener was the other option in [m56.md](phase-details/m56.md) and is not available at the cost it implies, because `srv.Run` blocks and is the last statement of `run`. Nothing between the probe and `ListenAndServe` ever read its result — the outbox retries regardless — so the ten seconds of dead server bought the order of two log lines and nothing else. Guarded by `TestTheSMTPProbeDoesNotGateTheListener` in `cmd/linkctrl`, which parses `main.go` and fails if the `Verify` call is not lexically inside a `go` statement; its blind spot is written into the test. The contradicted comment at `main.go:386-396` is true again, and `docs/configuration.md`'s `SMTP_TIMEOUT` row now says the probe is off the startup path |
| F75 | M44 | Revoking an API key is scoped to the key's id and its **owner**, with no organization check, while listing keys is scoped to the owner *and* the organization — so the two disagree about which keys an actor acting in one organization can reach | `internal/store/query/apikeys.sql`, `RevokeAPIKey` (`WHERE id = $1 AND user_id = $2`) against `ListAPIKeysForUser` (`WHERE user_id = $1 AND organization_id = $2`); the caller is `internal/auth/apikey.go` `Revoke`, which passes only `actor.UserID` | Read from the two statements, not reproduced. A user belonging to two organizations sees only the current organization's keys in the list, but a `DELETE /api/v1/api-keys/{id}` naming a key issued in the other one answers `204` and revokes it. Both keys are that user's own, so nothing crosses a tenancy boundary and no key is destroyed that its owner could not have destroyed by switching organization first. What it does give is a weak existence oracle — `204` versus `404` distinguishes *my key, elsewhere* from *not mine* — against ids the caller would have to guess. Not touched by M44: rotation adds no revoke path, and the milestone's org-wide keys are still revoked by their own owner | **Low.** An inconsistency between two statements of the same scoping rule, with a negligible oracle attached. The fix is one `AND organization_id = $3`, and the argument against it is that it would make a key unrevokable from the organization somebody is signed into, which is worse | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 7, low. **Re-homed 2026-08-05, owner-directed, and still open.** The owner's answer to this row is a change of model rather than a filter: a key minted by an *account*, reaching the organizations that account belongs to, GitHub-style. Validated as dissolving this row — revoke and list would both be owner-scoped, so they agree by construction instead of by a filter that makes a key unrevokable from the organization somebody is signed into, which this row's own severity calls worse than the defect. **Not built in M45**: it reverses [F103](#closed)'s bound, M44's `organization_id` parameter on `ResolveWorkspaceForUser`, D43's role cap and D87's rotation rule, so it is a tenancy model changing rather than a defect being fixed, and a phase close is not where that happens. Scheduled as a *Not in Phase 2* row in [Plan.md](../../Plan.md), which carries the work it implies. This row stays **open** until that lands: scheduled elsewhere is not resolved. **Scheduled 2026-08-06 as [M54](phase-details/m54.md)**, Phase 3 — which dissolves this row rather than filtering it, because revoking and listing both become owner-scoped. Still open until M54 lands. | **[M54](phase-details/m54.md)**, 2026-08-08 — **dissolved rather than filtered**, which is what the owner's 2026-08-05 direction bought. `ListAPIKeysForUser` lost `AND organization_id = $2` and now asks the same question `RevokeAPIKey` always asked: whose key is this. The two cannot disagree because there is one predicate, and an account-wide key has no organization to be filtered by at all. The `AND organization_id = $3` this row's own severity cell proposed and argued against was never written. `TestRevokingAndListingReachTheSameKeys` asserts the agreement on the list rather than on the oracle — the oracle was the symptom — and it was shown red by reinstating the organization predicate in `List`, which reported *a key the owner can revoke from here is absent from the list they see from here*. The cost is stated rather than hidden: somebody in two organizations now sees every key they own on one page instead of the current organization's, which is what a personal access token list looks like everywhere else. |
| F1 | 0.1.0 release | Release-notes extraction sweeps up the changelog's link-reference block | `.github/workflows/release.yml`, the "Extract this version's changelog section" step | The `awk` runs from `## [<version>]` to the next `## [` heading. The newest version is always the last section in `CHANGELOG.md`, so the trailing reference definitions are inside that range: the published v0.1.0 body carries `[Unreleased]: …` and `[0.1.0]: …` immediately before the `### Container image` section. Confirmed against the live release body, not inferred. | Cosmetic. Markdown renders link *definitions* as nothing, so a reader sees no artefact; the cruft is in the raw body only. It will recur on every release, because the newest section is last every time. | Yes — owner approved 2026-07-31, scheduled as [M45](phase-details/m45.md), which is the only milestone it can sit in: the extraction runs solely inside the release workflow, so the fix has to be checked against CHANGELOG.md as it will be at 0.2.0 before the tag exists rather than confirmed by publishing.  **Fix written and verified 2026-08-06, waiting on the owner to apply it.** It lands in `.github/workflows/release.yml`, which the agent's token may not push — see [W29](workflow-changes.md) and `ci/proposed/release.yml`. The row stays **open** rather than closed on a written fix: the extraction that runs at the 0.2.0 tag is the live workflow, not the proposal, so until it is applied the release body carries the link definitions exactly as v0.1.0's does.  **Corrected 2026-08-06, and it lowers the urgency I claimed.** The defect bites only the section that is *last* in `CHANGELOG.md`, because that is the only one whose range runs off the end into the reference block. v0.1.0 was hit because it was the first release and therefore last in the file. Once `[0.2.0]` was cut, `[0.1.0]` sits beneath it — and both the old program and the new one extract 0.2.0 with **zero** reference definitions, measured against the real file. Verified against the real `CHANGELOG.md` by running the live program over **both** released sections: 0.2.0 gives 1802 lines and 0.1.0 gives 164, each with zero stray reference definitions and no trailing blank. **The urgency I claimed was wrong and is corrected above**: the defect bites only whichever section is last in the file, so 0.2.0 was never going to carry them and this was defensive rather than blocking.
