| F64 | M33 | `ServeMux`'s own path-cleaning redirect is the one response on the redirect tree that carries none of the tree's headers — no `nosniff`, no `X-Robots-Tag`, no `Cache-Control` — and it serves an HTML body | `net/http`, `ServeMux.findHandler`; the rule it sits outside is stated at `internal/httpx/redirect.go`, `errorPage` — *"the redirect tree is outside the security-header chain, so every response it writes sets nosniff itself"* | Reproduced on the built image: `GET /ld1//deep` answers `307 Temporary Redirect`, `Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8`, `Location: /ld1/deep`, 45 bytes of HTML, and **none** of the three headers every other response on that tree sets. Same for `/ld1/../ld2` → `/ld2`. `ServeMux` cleans the escaped path before dispatching and answers the redirect itself, so no handler in this repository is involved and nothing the project writes can add headers to it. **Pre-existing rather than caused by M33** — `cleanPath` applies to every request whatever patterns are registered, so `/foo/../bar` behaved this way before the deep-link route existed. What M33 changes is how many paths reach it and end somewhere real: before, a cleaned two-segment path redirected and then 404ed. Verified **not** a 301, which would have broken the absolute *redirects are 302, never 301* rule: it is 307. The body is Go-escaped, so the reflected path is not an injection vector | **Minor.** No alias or destination is disclosed that the request did not already contain, and the missing header with any bite — `nosniff` on a `text/html` body — protects against sniffing content that is already HTML and already escaped. The cost is that the tree's stated invariant is not actually true of every response it produces, and the honest fix is either to wrap the mux so the headers are set on the way out or to amend the claim | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 4, minor — redirect path and cache. The batch owes one k6 re-run, not six. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. `alwaysNosniff` wraps every mux that can serve an alias — the split-host link mux, the custom-hostname mux, and the single-host mux — so `X-Content-Type-Options` is on the one response no handler in this repository writes: `ServeMux`'s own path-cleaning 307, which carries a `text/html` body. **Only nosniff, and that is a choice with a reason.** It is the missing header with any bite on an HTML body, and it is already set by every handler here and by the application tree's middleware, so applying it everywhere changes no response this project writes. `X-Robots-Tag` and `Cache-Control: no-store` are *error-page* policy rather than tree policy — `Location` deliberately sets a different `Cache-Control` and no robots header, because a short link is meant to be shared — so setting those in the wrapper would have changed successful redirects in order to fix a 307. The comment in `redirect.go` claiming the invariant now says what it covers. Shown red by removing the wrapper's one line. |
| F100 | M44.9 | `HEAD` advances a sequential split's durable rotation, re-phasing every subsequent visitor's arm with no click recorded | `internal/httpx/redirect_split.go:53` and `:127-136` never test `r.Method`, while `internal/httpx/redirect.go:454` (`record`) and `internal/httpx/redirect_gates.go:229-235` (budget) both do | Falsifies two absolutes in one decision entry — `decisions.md:9225-9231`, *"**The budget is the only gate that consumes something**"* and *"HEAD never consumes at all"* — because M36 put a second durable counter in the same table. A link checker or unfurler shifts the rotation on every probe, and because no click row is written the per-destination breakdown cannot show why the arms are uneven. The companion claim, that a deep link the alias cannot forward also spends its position, is **not** a finding: D57 records that deliberately at `redirect_split.go:114-121`, `decisions.md:9406-9413` and `Plan.md:810` | Minor. Skews a split test under crawler traffic; no visitor sees a wrong page and nothing is destroyed. Fix note: returning early on HEAD makes a HEAD answer the link's *own* destination while a GET answers an arm, so a checker validates a URL no visitor is sent to — HEAD must still choose an arm without advancing, which `NextVariantRotation` cannot do | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 3, minor — a principal can act on it, in scope by the 2026-08-04 standing rule. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. `PeekVariantRotation` and `gate.PeekRotation`: a HEAD chooses the arm the next GET would get and does not advance the durable counter. The row's own fix note ruled out the obvious repair and it was right — returning early on HEAD would have a checker validate the link's *own* destination, a URL no visitor is ever sent to — so HEAD had to keep choosing, which `NextVariantRotation` could not do. The read-only twin is **Budget's shape exactly**, for the same caller and the same reason: HEAD must be answered with what a visitor would get and must not change it. No row means position 1, which is the first arm. The two absolutes this row convicted in D8's entry are now true again. Shown red by taking the HEAD branch out: five probes moved the rotation from 0 to 5. |
| F115 | M44.9 | D54's per-alias password limiter has an availability cost nobody recorded, and a correct password spends a token | `internal/httpx/redirect_gates.go:161` calls `passwordAllowed` before `ParseForm` and before verification; `:204-216` keys the second limb on the alias with both limbs consumed on refusal; `LINK_PASSWORD_RATE_LIMIT` defaults to 20 refilling one per three seconds | The keying itself is D54's recorded choice and arguing it is re-litigation — the decision reasons explicitly that *"an attacker driving attempts through many visitors' browsers spreads them across addresses and walks past a per-address limit"*, and consuming both limbs on refusal is documented in the function's own doc. What is **not** recorded: D54 states the fail-open cost and no other, so nothing anywhere says a stranger can hold a password link's bucket empty and lock out its intended audience at roughly one request every three seconds. And verification found a limb nobody filed — because the limiter runs before the form is parsed, a link with more than twenty legitimate visitors in a burst throttles itself with no attacker present | Minor as a defect, since the mechanism was chosen deliberately; the row is for the missing risk note against D54 and the self-throttling limb. Fix note: dropping the alias limb or keying it on address-plus-alias reopens the distributed-guessing hole D54 exists to close, and D53's CSRF waiver is load-bearing on D54 — so any change here reopens D53. Refunding the alias token on a **correct** password touches neither decision | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 3, minor — a principal can act on it, in scope by the 2026-08-04 standing rule. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. **The self-throttling limb is fixed and the other one is recorded**, which is the split the row itself draws. A correct password refunds the per-alias token — `Limiter.RefundKey`, with the shared script clamping to burst so a negative cost cannot inflate a bucket — and that touches neither D53 nor D54, because the keying that closes distributed guessing is unchanged, an attacker without the password never reaches the refund, and what is returned was spent by somebody who proved they had it. The **address** limb stays spent deliberately: a correct password is still traffic from that address. The measurement had to be taken on the alias bucket directly, because a fixture has one address and the per-address limb refuses first — which is itself the reason the defect is invisible from a single client. The limb this does **not** close is D54's accepted cost, now written against the decision rather than left to be discovered: a stranger can hold the bucket empty with wrong guesses at roughly one request every three seconds. |
| F116 | M44.9 | With `forward_path` on, the deep-link remainder is visitor-controlled, unbounded to 1 MiB, and walked or copied several times per request | `internal/httpx/redirect.go:721-761`; `internal/platform/httpserver/server.go:43-56` sets `Addr`, `Handler`, `ReadHeaderTimeout`, `WriteTimeout` and `ErrorLog` and never `MaxHeaderBytes` | No length check exists between the router and the joiner. `joinable` walks every `/`-separated segment calling `url.PathUnescape`, then the concatenation, the unescape of the join and `u.String()` each copy the whole remainder — roughly five passes over up to a megabyte, on a request that touches no database. A cache hit is never charged to the 404-probe limiter, and `docs/slo.md:913-917` records that as deliberate | Minor, corrected down from the tier it was filed at: it is CPU and allocation amplification rather than disclosure or corruption, it needs `forward_path` switched on per link, and 1 MiB is a real ceiling rather than none. Fix note: capping total length alone misses it, because `joinable`'s per-segment unescape is driven by segment **count** — a 40 KiB path of 20,000 empty segments is 20,000 allocations. Bound both, and refuse through the existing not-forwardable 404 rather than inventing a 414 on a tree whose header invariants F64 already shows are fragile | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 3, minor — a principal can act on it, in scope by the 2026-08-04 standing rule. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. `MaxForwardedPath` (4096) and `MaxForwardedSegments` (64), checked in `joinable`. **Both, because either alone misses it** — the row's own fix note, and it is right: length alone leaves the per-segment cost, which is driven by segment *count*, so a 40 KiB path of twenty thousand empty segments is twenty thousand `PathUnescape` calls under any sane length cap. Refused through the existing not-forwardable 404 rather than a 414, as the row asked, because a new status on this tree would tell a prober something the 404 does not — and the tree's header invariants were fragile enough that F64 was open against them at the time. Generous on purpose: an amplification bound, not a validation rule. |
| F50 | M32.9 | With bot blocking on, a blocked bot writes a click event for **expired and archived** links, which recorded nothing before M32.5 | `internal/httpx/redirect.go:188-198` (the gate calls `h.record`) against `:208-216` (`OutcomeGone` returns without recording) | Reproduced both directions on one link pair. Blocking off: expired 410, archived 404, `clicks=0`, `click_count=0`. Blocking on: both 403, `expired clicks=6 archived clicks=3`, and `click_count` moved to match. Analytics are unaffected — every recorded row carries `is_bot`, and `link_click_daily` non-bot clicks stayed 0 — but `links.click_count` is rendered raw at `links_table.html:29` and `dashboard.html:45` and is the `sort=clicks` key, so an archived link can accrue a visible count from crawler traffic and outrank active links. No milestone claim is falsified: m32.5.md:64 states *"A blocked attempt is counted, not audited"* unconditionally. The direction is safe — `click_count > 0` gates both `ReserveAlias` and `PurgeExpiredLinks`, so both alias-protection paths become more protective | **Minor.** An unstated consequence of a stated rule rather than a contradiction. Identical traffic is recorded or not depending on a setting about *responses*; the honest fix is a decision — record for every state or none — not a correction. F24's family, extended to links that previously counted nothing | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 6, minor — wrong behaviour nothing can provoke. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05, [D101](../../Plan.md#phase-2-decisions-taken-after-the-plan-was-finalised), owner-answered. Recorded **in every state** rather than in none: `OutcomeGone` now records, and so does `OutcomeNotFound` when a real link is behind it — a link that exists and was asked for a path it cannot forward — while an alias that does not exist still records nothing, because there is no link to attribute a click to. The row is right that this was a decision and not a correction: no milestone claim was falsified, since m32.5.md says *"a blocked attempt is counted, not audited"* without qualification, and that sentence is untouched. *Record for none* was rejected with a reason — it makes the redirect path decide what to record from the response it was about to send, which is coupling the current shape avoids, and leaves a blocked bot on a live link counted, so the rule gains an exception instead of losing one. `TestAnExpiredLinkRecordsItsTrafficWithOrWithoutBotBlocking` puts two expired links side by side, one blocking and one not, and asserts both record; shown red by removing the call. **What this deliberately does not fix** is the surprise the row opens with: archived links go on accruing visible counts from crawlers, because `links.click_count` is rendered raw beside a rollup that counts only humans — that is [F24](#closed), approved separately. |
| F105 | M44.9 | `notifications.workspace_id` is written on every M40 and M43 notification and read by nothing, while two comments state it produces a per-workspace inbox | `internal/store/query/notifications.sql:16`, `:31` and `:52` all key on `user_id` alone; `internal/notify/notify.go:483-501` (`toNotification`) never copies the column and `Notification` at `:67-78` has no field for it | `internal/notify/domains.go:45-47` and `internal/notify/automation.go:32-33` both assert, verbatim, that *"The notification carries the owning workspace so it appears in that workspace's inbox rather than wherever the reader happens to be standing."* No read path consults it, so it cannot reach any surface. `internal/notify/automation.go` also writes a body reading *"matched … in **this workspace**"* that the reader cannot resolve | Minor. Nothing leaks and nothing fails; the cost is a column written on every notification with no reader, and two comments that would stop the next person adding the filter. It is also the stated mitigation for F94 — its authors believed cross-workspace notifications were contained by an inbox scope that does not exist. Fix note: a naive `AND workspace_id = @ws` hides every organization-level notification, since disputes and audit-growth write NULL; the predicate must be `IS NULL OR = @ws`, and it must be added identically to the count and the preview or `notifications_user_unread_idx` stops serving them, which `notifications.sql:26-28` warns about | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05, [D102](../../Plan.md#phase-2-decisions-taken-after-the-plan-was-finalised), owner-answered. The filter the two comments had been describing since M40 is built: `workspace_id IS NULL OR workspace_id = @workspace_id`, added **identically** to `ListNotifications`, `CountUnreadNotifications` and `ListUnreadNotificationPreview`, so `notifications_user_unread_idx` keeps serving the count and the preview and the badge cannot disagree with the page it previews. `Notification` gains the field and `toNotification` copies it; `api/openapi.yaml` declares it. Both comments now say when they became true and that they were not before. **Built rather than deleted because of [F94](#closed)**, which was closed believing cross-workspace notifications were contained by an inbox scope that did not exist — deleting the column would have settled that row's question in the other direction. `TestTheInboxIsScopedToTheWorkspaceExceptWhereItIsNot` asserts both halves and was shown red twice: with no scoping at all, and with the `IS NULL` half removed, which hid every organization-level notification and is the failure the row's fix note predicted. |
| F44 | M32.9 | No account deletion or GDPR erasure exists anywhere, while the schema and four other sites describe both as behaviour that exists — and two tables holding email addresses have no deletion path of any kind | `internal/store/migrations/00200_identity.sql:35-37` (`anonymized_at`, *"Set by the GDPR erasure routine"*), and `users` / `destination_disputes` | `anonymized_at` has no writer: grep returns the column, the sqlc model field and three `SELECT *` scan lists. `users` appears in none of the 14 `DELETE FROM` statements and nothing writes `users.deleted_at`. Four further sites assert deletion as existing: `docs/SECURITY.md:51`, `internal/audit/audit.go:277`, `01200_invitations.sql:38` and `01600_destination_disputes.sql:56`. The same migration marks `mfa_secret` *"Phase 3"* four lines earlier, so the file knows how to mark something forward-looking and did not here. Two corrections to the finder's enumeration: `audit_logs` **is** purgeable — it is in `PartitionedTables` and governed by `NewRetentionPolicy`, just defaulted to 0 and documented that way in three places — and `invitations` and `notifications` **do** cascade from `organizations`/`users`. What is genuinely undeletable is `users`, and `destination_disputes`, which by explicit design has *no foreign keys at all* (`01600:49-52`) and no purge. `reserved_aliases` also lacks a purge but holds no personal data | **Low to moderate.** Nothing exploitable. Its cost is that the project's own convention — every deferral is written down — has a hole exactly where a compliance reader would look: erasure appears in neither Plan.md's scope rows nor *Not in Phase 2*. `Plan.md:426`'s framing (*"the largest table … is out of scope for subject-access and erasure requests"*) reads as though the other tables are in scope | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 2, minor to moderate. Closes as a **record and not a build** — the finding is that erasure is described by five sites and implemented by none, and saying so is the fix, the way [F141](#closed) said it. Building erasure is a feature and belongs in Plan.md first. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. **Recorded, not built**, as the triage decided: writing an erasure subsystem inside a phase close is a feature and belongs in Plan.md first. All five sites that described erasure in the present tense are corrected — `00200_identity.sql`'s *"set by the GDPR erasure routine"* on a column with no writer, `00400_analytics.sql`'s scope sentence which was true of its own table and read as a claim about the others, `00600_phase2_dormant.sql`'s *"GDPR erasure overwrites the label"*, `internal/audit/audit.go`'s *"after the account is deleted"*, and `docs/SECURITY.md`'s copy of the same. The absence itself is now in the two lists a reader consults — Plan.md's *Not in Phase 2* and README's *Not built yet* — and has its own entry under SECURITY.md's *What is not defended*, each naming what **is** reclaimed (partitioned analytics, audit retention, invitation and notification cascades) so the gap is sized rather than gestured at. The row's own correction to the finder's enumeration is preserved: `audit_logs` is purgeable and the cascades do work. **Scheduled 2026-08-06 as [M52](phase-details/m52.md)**, Phase 3 — which also answers the three dormant columns this row rests on: `deleted_at` and `anonymized_at` get writers, and `suspended` deliberately does not. **Built by [M52](phase-details/m52.md) on 2026-08-08, and this row is now closed on both limbs.** `DELETE /api/v1/account` and a form on the account page delete the calling account in one transaction; an hourly sweep scrubs `audit_logs.actor_label` and **both** of `destination_disputes`'s label columns to a constant tombstone and stamps `anonymized_at` (D148). The two tables this row named as having no deletion path now have one each, of the right kind: `users` is soft-deleted and scrubbed, and `destination_disputes` keeps its rows and loses its people, which is what its own design note asked for. `suspended` still has no writer and a test says so. **One limb the fix does not reach** is recorded rather than quietly left: an address inside an audit record's `metadata` survives erasure, which is [F177](#closed). |
| F57 | M32.9 | The shared rate limiter writes the client's full IPv4 address into a Redis key, and nothing tells an operator pointing at a managed Redis that they are now storing addresses | `internal/ratelimit/ratelimit.go:299-310` (`Key`), composed at `internal/ratelimit/shared.go:175` | Confirmed live, read-only, on the running instance: `redis-cli --scan --pattern 'lc:rl:*'` → `lc:rl:api:172.18.0.1`, TTL 48s of a computed 120s. Two counters land and reframe it: a Redis key is not a column, so the stance (*"No IP column anywhere"*) is not literally violated — every corroborating doc scopes itself to storage; and the `/64` for IPv6 is evasion resistance, not privacy (`Key`'s own doc: *"Handing out /64s to single hosts is normal"*), so anonymising the v4 side to /24 is **not** an available fix — it would let one host throttle 255 neighbours. Keyed hashing is the only coherent fix. What survives: the shipped compose is memory-only, but `LINKCTRL_REDIS_URL` is operator-configurable and managed Redis commonly snapshots by default | **Low.** Bounded to 120 seconds in a store the design treats as disposable. The actionable half is documentation: the privacy stance should say what it does about Redis, and `docs/deployment.md`'s managed-Redis guidance should tell operators to disable persistence | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 7, low. Closes as a **recorded limitation and not code** — its own fix note says keyed hashing is the only coherent repair, and the actionable half is telling an operator pointing at managed Redis what is stored there and for how long. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. **Documentation, which the row itself says is the actionable half.** The inherited privacy stance now states its own exception: it is about storage, and the shared limiter's Redis key carries the full IPv4 address for the limiter's window. `docs/deployment.md` tells an operator pointing at a managed Redis to disable persistence there, with the reason — a service snapshotting by default writes client addresses to somebody else's disk on a retention schedule the operator did not choose. Both texts say why anonymising the v4 side is **not** available: a `/24` key would let one host exhaust 255 neighbours' budget, and the `/64` used for IPv6 is evasion resistance rather than privacy. Keyed hashing is named as the only coherent fix and is not built. |
| F58 | M32.9 | `SeedBlocklist` accepts malformed `LINKCTRL_DESTINATION_BLOCKLIST` entries silently, inserting rows that can never match | `internal/link/blocking.go:688-708` — lowercase, trim, insert; `checkListEntry` is never called | Reproduced: `HostCandidates` only ever produces dot-delimited fragments of a real hostname, so a stored `https://evil.example` or `*.evil.example` row is unreachable by `MatchBlockedDestination`. Two narrowings. The panic-on-malformed promise at `blocking.go:170-173` is scoped, in both places it appears, to the **embedded** list — generalising it to the env list is inference. And it is not a regression: the pre-M30 in-memory implementation validated nothing either, which is what m30.md's only claim here (*"the env list keeps working"*) promises. The implied fix is also wrong: calling `checkListEntry` would reject IP-literal entries that currently work | **Low.** Operator-authored and self-inflicted; it cannot make the instance refuse less than before M30. The cost is silence — the operator sees their entry in the table and it refuses nothing — one function call away from the boot-time rigour `main.go:508-517` spends on exactly this failure mode | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 7, low. Closes as a **recorded limitation and not code** — calling `checkListEntry` would reject IP-literal entries that work today, which this row's own evidence names. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. Recorded at `SeedBlocklist` and in `docs/configuration.md`, where an operator writing the variable will meet it: bare hostnames, not URLs or wildcards, because `https://evil.example` and `*.evil.example` pass the host fold, land in the table, appear in the list and match nothing. **Calling `checkListEntry` is the wrong fix and the comment says so**: it refuses IP-literal entries, which work today and which an operator blocking `169.254.169.254` reasonably expects to — so the cheap repair trades a silent no-op for a silent regression. Also not a regression from anything: the pre-M30 in-memory list validated nothing either. |
| F113 | M44.9 | Nothing reaps an unverified hostname claim, so a registration holds a name instance-wide forever | `internal/link/verification.go:339-343` skips rows with `VerifiedAt == nil`; `domains_hostname_key` (`00300_links.sql:23`) is instance-wide and unconditioned on verification | Narrowed sharply by verification: the squat itself is a **documented limitation** — `Plan.md:815` states *"a second registration of the same name is refused instance-wide, whoever asks… no way to hand one over"* — and the uninformative refusal is deliberate and reasoned in-tree. The index is partial on `deleted_at IS NULL`, so deleting the domain does free the name, but only the holder can delete it. What is genuinely undocumented is that an unverified claim is never polled out of existence, so the squat is free and permanent rather than merely unhandable | Low. No confidentiality or integrity loss; denial of a name on one instance, and the operator's own hostnames are configured statically rather than through this table. Fix note: a reaper on a timer breaks the case `verification.go:339-343` protects — a hostname registered before DNS cut-over — and must not treat a NULL `verification_checked_at` as evidence of abandonment, since `RenameDomain` writes that NULL on a live row | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 7, low. Closes as a **recorded limitation and not code** — a reaper on unverified claims breaks the pre-DNS-cut-over case `verification.go:339-343` protects. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. Recorded at `verificationWorkList`, which is the function that skips these rows, and in Plan.md's hostname limitation beside the squat it already documented. The finding is the half that was **not** documented: the claim is not merely unhandable, it is permanent, because nothing polls an unverified registration out of existence. **A reaper is not the fix it looks like** — the pending arm exists to keep checking a hostname registered before its DNS cut-over, which is exactly what a reaper would delete, and it could not treat a NULL `verification_checked_at` as abandonment because `RenameDomain` writes one onto a live row. |
| F117 | M44.9 | `canAdminister`'s organization limb authorizes an organization-owned domain from the identity's permission union rather than the covering membership | `internal/link/domains.go:131-143`, the `organizationID != nil` limb, which compares only `actor.OrgID` after a bare `actor.Can(PermDomainsWrite)` | This is D44's rule unapplied — *"a write is authorized by the membership whose scope covers the object being written, not by the identity's union"* — so a workspace-scoped admin's union would reach an organization-owned row. Latency verified on four independent checks: `CreateDomain`'s only caller writes both owner columns, no update statement touches `workspace_id`, the seeded default row has a NULL organization, and `domains.workspace_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting a workspace removes the row rather than promoting it | Low and latent — the state is unconstructible through any shipped or migrated path, and the CHECK at `02500_domain_ownership.sql:48-52` is what legalises it. It becomes real the moment anything registers an organization-owned hostname, and whoever adds that surface has no reason to look here. Fix note: replacing all three limbs with the organization-wide check is wrong — only the middle limb is D44's, the workspace limb is correctly the union under D31, and the instance-default limb is F70 and D38's unanswered question about who administers an instance. Also `Domains()` calls this per row, so a per-call authority load is a query per row | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 7, low. Closes as a **recorded limitation and not code** — the state is unconstructible through any shipped or migrated path, and the fix note rules out replacing all three limbs. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. Recorded in `canAdminister`, beside the limb it is about, because the row's own point is that whoever adds an organization-owned hostname surface has no reason to look here. The comment carries the four checks that make it latent rather than live — `CreateDomain`'s only caller writes both owner columns, no update statement touches `workspace_id`, the seeded default has a NULL organization, and `workspace_id` is `ON DELETE CASCADE` so deleting a workspace removes the row rather than promoting it — and why the obvious repair is wrong: replacing all three limbs takes the workspace limb with it, and that one is correctly the union under D31. Untouched by [D100](../../Plan.md#phase-2-decisions-taken-after-the-plan-was-finalised), which moved the *instance-default* limb and left this one exactly as filed. |
| F70 | M39 | `domains.write` is a **role** permission, so on an instance with more than one organization every organization's owner and admin can administer the **instance-wide** default domain — its root redirect and its bot policy — not only their own hostnames | `internal/store/migrations/00800_domain_root_redirect.sql:32-34` grants the slug to the owner and admin role ids with no organization scope; `internal/link/domains.go`, `canAdminister`'s third limb returns `domains.write` alone for a row with both owner columns NULL; `internal/link/domain_settings.go:77` and `:191` guard `SetRootRedirect` and `SetBotBlocking` on the bare permission | Named while writing M39's ownership check, which had to decide what the instance default's limb should be. m39.md says the default "**stays** administrable only by instance owner/admin", so preserving the existing guard is what the milestone asked for and M39 neither widens nor narrows it — registering a hostname is not a route to it, and the new collection refuses to rename or delete the default at all. The reach is nonetheless real and already documented from the other end: `Plan.md`'s known-limitation row for the dispute queue calls it "the shape `domains.write` already has", `docs/SECURITY.md:174` repeats it, and migration `01600_destination_disputes.sql:100-105` excluded admin from `destinations.review` for exactly this reason. Recorded rather than fixed because narrowing it is a change to who administers an instance — the missing instance-level principal (D38) — rather than a defect in per-domain ownership | **Minor**, and unreachable on a single-organization instance, which is every default one. It becomes real with `LINKCTRL_SIGNUP_MODE=open`, where one registration provisions an organization and makes the registrant its owner: they then hold `domains.write` and can repoint the root of the hostname every workspace's links are served on. The same registration already reaches `destination_disputes`' sibling gap (F15), so the two want one answer rather than two | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 3, minor — a principal can act on it, in scope by the 2026-08-04 standing rule. **Blocked on D38**, the missing instance-level principal that also blocked F15 and F31 on the first triage. D98 and `lctl instance principal move` moved that ground since D38 was written, so the question returns in a different shape rather than staying refused, and it is put when this row is reached. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05, [D100](../../Plan.md#phase-2-decisions-taken-after-the-plan-was-finalised), owner-answered. A new permission, `domains.write.instance` (migration `03500`), granted to **no role** and reaching a person only through `instance_grants` — so the instance default domain's root redirect and bot policy are the instance principal's. `domains.write` is untouched and still the ownership check over a workspace's own hostnames, which is M39's point and was never what this row was about. D18 limb recorded as the inherited rule requires: **neither** — repointing the root escalates nothing, is reversible, and discloses nothing, so it is delegable and deliberately absent from `NonDelegableScopes`. Also absent from `InstanceGrantable`: the principal holds it and may not confer it, because what D98 decided the principal may delegate is instance-level *review*. **Two things surfaced while building it.** `canAdminister`'s instance-default limb is now checked first, before the role permission, since checking `domains.write` up front let a workspace admin past the gate and left the answer to the switch — the arrangement that made this reachable. And `domainForWrite`'s *this is the instance default* refusal moved **above** the permission check: it is a fact about the row rather than the actor, nobody may rename or delete it including the principal, and until now every owner passed the permission check and reached it anyway, so the two orders were indistinguishable. The migration confers the scope on **whoever already holds the principal** rather than re-deriving the earliest account, so a principal moved with `lctl instance principal move` stays moved. `TestTheInstanceRootIsThePrincipalsAndNotAnOrganizationOwners` replaces the old *needs domains.write* test, whose premise D100 falsified — instance grants survive a demotion, so demoting the fixture owner no longer removes the capability — and it asserts a full organization **owner** is refused, which is the finding stated as a test. Shown red by reverting the guard. |
| F12 | M28.5 | D18's text does not describe the map it governs, in two ways: applied literally its two limbs would make an irreversible permission delegable, and its *"either direction"* sentence promises a disarming the code never performs | [Plan.md](../../Plan.md#phase-2-decisions-taken-after-the-plan-was-finalised)'s D18 row, against `auth.NonDelegableScopes` at `internal/auth/apikey.go:186` and the comment stating the wider rule at `:131-143`; enforcement at `resolveScopes` (`internal/auth/apikey.go:607`, the mint path) and `narrowScopes` (`internal/auth/apikey_rotation.go:320-338`, the rotation path), against `Authenticate` (`:515-598`), whose `identity.restrictTo(row.Scopes)` at `:589` re-filters on the owner's role and nothing else | Found while recording `org.delete`'s delegability, which m28.5.md required be named against D18. D18 says a permission is non-delegable "when reading it exposes an actor's identity tied to network data, **or** when holding it lets a key widen its own reach. Everything else is delegable." `org.delete` does neither — it discloses nothing and removes tenancy rather than granting any — yet it has been in `NonDelegableScopes` since Phase 1's `00700_seed.sql`, on irreversibility. The map's own comment states the wider rule explicitly: "the rule this map encodes is now 'escalating, irreversible, or disclosing' rather than only the first two." So the code and the decision text disagree about what the rule *is*, and the code is the one that has been right since before D18 was written (2026-07-31). **Folded in from F56 (M32.9), 2026-08-04:** the same decision's other sentence is wrong the other way. D18 says *"Flipping a permission in **either direction** stays a one-line map edit"*, and it does not: adding an already-granted permission to `NonDelegableScopes` would not disarm keys already issued, because `Authenticate` restricts the identity to the **row's** stored scopes and never consults the map. Never exploitable — `git log -S` confirms all the entries landed in the same commit as the migration creating their permission (`a0f23ff` for `audit.read`/00900, `e8a6b1c` for `destinations.review`/01600, the Phase-1 seed for the rest), so no key has ever held a scope that later became non-delegable. F56's own "only enforcement" reading is now **one site out of date** and is corrected here rather than carried forward: M44's rotation added a second check, `narrowScopes` at `apikey_rotation.go:328-338`, which refuses to carry a now-non-delegable scope into a successor. Mint and rotate are covered; request time is not. F56's cited claim at `apikey.go:147-149` was an over-read — that comment is about the *deleting* direction and is true there. | Minor today, and latent rather than active: no permission is mis-classified in the tree, and `org.delete` is correctly non-delegable. The cost is the next milestone that adds an irreversible permission — M44's rotation scopes are the near candidate — applying D18's two limbs as written, finding neither matches, and concluding the scope is delegable. Correcting it means rewording a decision, which is the owner's, and D18 is referenced by name from six milestone files. Absorbs F56's limb, which was filed **Note / informational** and stays that: no defect in shipped behaviour, and the fix is either an accurate one-line correction to D18 or an `isNonDelegable` filter in `restrictTo` as belt-and-braces — the helper already exists at `internal/auth/apikey.go:675-678` and is called from the two write paths but not from the read one. Both limbs are corrections to the same decision's text, which is why they are one row. | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. **[F56](#closed) (M32.9) was merged into this row** by M45's queue hygiene, 2026-08-04: both convict D18's wording against the same map, and one reword answers both. **[F104](#closed) was deliberately not merged** although it calls itself *"F12's shape a second time"* — same shape, different rule, different file, different code: correcting D18 does nothing for `phase-details/README.md:75`, and merging on shape alone would put two unrelated repairs behind one disposition. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05, owner-answered. **The text is corrected and the map is untouched**, which is the direction this row argued for: the code has been right since before D18 was written. Limb one now reads *escalating, irreversible, or disclosing* — the three-limb rule `NonDelegableScopes`'s own comment has stated all along — so `org.delete` is described by the rule that has governed it since Phase 1. Limb two is **narrowed rather than deleted**: adding a scope to the map binds keys minted and rotated afterwards and does not disarm one already issued, because `Authenticate` restricts to the row's stored scopes and never consults the map. The request-time gap is **documented and left open**, with the price of closing it stated — an `isNonDelegable` filter in `restrictTo` is a check on every authenticated request for a state no key has ever been in. Both corrections are marked in place in the D18 row rather than replacing it, and the near miss is recorded at the map too: the next milestone to add an irreversible permission is the one that would have applied the two limbs, found neither matched, and shipped it delegable. Absorbs F56's limb. No `D` number: decisions.md is append-only and a later entry correcting an earlier one is what it is for. |
| F69 | M38 | [phase-details/README.md](phase-details/README.md)'s inherited-rules table says `demoCoverage()`'s **"four trailing rows"** assert zero for milestones not yet built. There are three | `docs/build-notes/phase-details/README.md`, the *A new feature somebody can see* row — against `cmd/lctl/demo_coverage_test.go`, whose trailing `MaxIsZero` rows are M41, M42 and M43 | Counted while adding M38's coverage rows. `git show 3ffebb0:cmd/lctl/demo_coverage_test.go` has four trailing boundary rows — M34, M41, M42, M43 — so the sentence was true when M33.5 wrote it; M34 converted its own row when it landed, exactly as the rule instructs, and the count beside the rule was not moved with it. (A fourth `MaxIsZero` row exists at `:201`, but it is M32's "no reputation feed verdict anywhere" assertion rather than a boundary row, so a bare grep count still reads four and hides this) | **Cosmetic, with a small teeth.** Nothing fails and no milestone is misled about what it must do. What it costs is that the number is the only thing telling a reader how many milestones are still unbuilt-and-watched, so it will drift again at M41 and there is nothing that notices. The durable fix is to stop writing the count — say "its trailing rows" — rather than to correct it to three. **Tier restated 2026-08-04 (M45) and held at Cosmetic.** The widening moves the number from three to zero, not the class: still nothing fails and still no milestone is misled. What it does change is the standing of the *teeth* — this row predicted the count would drift again at M41 with nothing to notice, and it then drifted at M41, M42, M43 and M44 unnoticed, so the durable fix it proposes (stop writing the count) is now demonstrated rather than argued. | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 8, cosmetic and trivial. **Widened by [M44.9](phase-details/m44.9.md), 2026-08-04:** the count is now zero, not three. Every trailing zero-asserting boundary row was converted as its milestone landed — M39/M40 at `cmd/lctl/demo_coverage_test.go:429`, M41 at `:474`, M42 at `:518`, M43 at `:566`, M44 at `:638` — so `phase-details/README.md:80`'s *"Its four trailing rows"* now describes nothing that exists. This row predicted it would drift again at M41 with nothing to notice; it then drifted at M41, M42, M43 and M44, and by M44.9 the true count was **zero**, so the sentence described nothing that existed. | F168 | M47 | **`make demo-update` fails permanently once the second workspace's links have landed in the wrong workspace, and the reset cannot recover it.** The demo instance is stuck: two consecutive runs failed at the same statement, and no re-run can clear it, because the rows that block the seed are in a workspace the reset does not reach | `cmd/lctl/demo_phase2.go:1796-1798` (the reset deletes the second workspace by `demoWorkspace2Slug`, so it reaches only links *inside* it) and `:529-552` (`seedSecondWorkspace` creates `demoWorkspace2Catalogue()`'s five aliases through an actor switched into that workspace) | Reproduced twice, 2026-08-07, against the demo instance at `b01f512`: `lctl: create Campaigns links: create "spring-webinar": conflict: alias "spring-webinar" is already in use on default`. The demo database holds `spring-webinar`, `roadshow`, `partner-portal` and `cw-survey` — four of `demoWorkspace2Catalogue()`'s five — in the **Default** workspace (`019fc2d4-69a4-7d8a-8093-52799bcd5a59`, 26 links) while the freshly created **Campaigns** workspace holds **zero**. Aliases are unique per *domain*, not per workspace, and both workspaces are on the default domain, so the Default copies block the Campaigns creation and the reset — scoped to the `campaigns` slug — never sees them. A second, independent instance of the same shape is visible beside it: a **`Marketing` workspace (slug `marketing`, created 2026-08-02)** is still in the demo's organization with zero links, because the reset matches slug `campaigns` and a rename of `demoWorkspace2Name`/`demoWorkspace2Slug` orphans whatever the previous name created. **Not caused by M47**, and that was checked rather than assumed: `git diff 099b561 HEAD` touches `internal/httpx/web_links.go` and three test files and nothing under `cmd/lctl/`, `internal/link` or `internal/team`. What M47 did was be the next milestone to run the gate | **Major, and it blocks the loop rather than the product.** Nothing an operator runs is affected — this is the demo seeder — but `make demo-update` is [phase-loop.md](phase-loop.md#3-land) step 3.7 and a milestone cannot land while it fails, so every subsequent milestone stops at the same place. The demo instance is also now one milestone behind and showing M46's build. Two things are wrong and they are separable: the reset cannot clean up after a workspace rename (the `Marketing` relic), and links that should be in the second workspace are in the first, which nothing asserts | **No — raised 2026-08-07, unreviewed.** It stopped the M47 landing at 3.7, so it is reported with the run rather than left for the next drain | **Fixed 2026-08-07**, in its own commit rather than inside a milestone, on owner approval given when it stopped [M47](phase-details/m47.md) at step 3.7. Two edits. `demoReset` now collects `demoWorkspace2Catalogue()`'s aliases as well as the main catalogue's (`cmd/lctl/demo_data.go`), so the organization-scoped delete reaches those five wherever they sit rather than relying on the workspace delete to cascade them. And the workspace delete became `slug <> 'default'` rather than `slug = demoWorkspace2Slug` (`cmd/lctl/demo_phase2.go`), so a rename of the second workspace stops orphaning what the old name made — which is what left `Marketing` standing since 2026-08-02. Verified against the broken instance itself rather than a fresh one: the jammed demo database recovered with no manual SQL, and two consecutive seeds produced the same demo. The `Marketing` relic is gone. **This row's second half did not close with it** and is [F169](#closed): the links are still created in the wrong workspace, and the reset now recovers from a state that should not arise. |
| F130 | M44.9 | The guard that closed F39 has nothing holding it in place — the fix's own branches execute against zero inputs | `internal/httpx/web_shell_test.go:196-200` (recording an embedded field as declared) and `:111-140` (`declaredNames` walking non-shell promotions) | **Proved by performing the revert**: deleting the three lines that record an embedded field leaves `TestNoPageDataStructShadowsTheShell` and the whole `internal/httpx` package green. An AST scan of all 25 non-test files in the package confirms every page struct embeds exactly `shell` and nothing else, so the recursive walk never fires. One premise corrected — `embeddedName`'s package-qualifier stripping *does* run, via `internal/httpx/timing.go:67` and `web_keys.go:16`, though neither struct embeds the shell so the names are computed and discarded; the pointer branch has genuinely zero inputs anywhere. F39's sabotage was shown red and then deleted along with the file that carried it | Minor. No live defect — the guard is correct today — but it is F39's own finding one level up: a mechanism believed to work with nothing that fails if it stops. The comment at `:31-41` names three shapes it defends against and the package contains none of them. Sibling of F40, which is the analogous gap in the theme guard | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. Deliberately **not merged** with [F40](#closed) by M45's queue hygiene, 2026-08-04, despite the sibling framing this row and F121 both use. Different guard, different package, different failure mode: F40 is a scan whose root excludes files the rule covers, this is a test whose branches have no inputs at all. No single edit closes both. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. The guard now has inputs. `TestTheShellGuardCatchesEachShapeItClaimsTo` runs `readStruct`, `declaredNames` and `embedsShell` over synthetic package source carrying the four shapes their comments claim to defend against — a named field shadowing a shell field, an embedded type whose *name* is one, an embedded pointer to a qualified type, and a field promoted through a struct that does not reach shell — plus the arrangement the guard must **not** flag, a page reaching shell through an intermediate. Synthetic rather than a wider scan on purpose: the package contains none of these shapes, which is the whole finding, so nothing read off the tree could exercise the branches. Verified by performing F130's own revert — deleting the three lines that record an embedded field — which used to leave the package green and now fails two cases by name. |
| F16 | M32 | Most dashboard pages are counted as redirects in the HTTP metrics, because the surface classifier's prefix list stopped being updated after Phase 1 | `internal/observability/metrics.go` — `webPrefixes`, and `ClassifySurface`'s fall-through, which returns `SurfaceRedirect` for anything it does not recognise | Noticed while adding `/feeds` and finding there was no list to add it to that anybody had been maintaining. `webPrefixes` holds eight entries — `/login`, `/logout`, `/setup`, `/dashboard`, `/docs`, `/links`, `/keys`, `/account` — and `ClassifySurface` labels everything else that is not `/api/`, `/static/` or an ops endpoint as `redirect`. Every dashboard route added since Phase 1 is therefore mislabelled: `/notifications` (M22), `/workspaces` and `/members` (M25, M28), `/invites` and `/invite/` (M27), `/organizations` (M28), `/signup` and `/verify/` (M29), `/disputes` (M31), `/theme` and `/workspace/` — and `/feeds` would be the twelfth. `dashboardPatterns` in `internal/httpx/router.go` is the list that *is* maintained, and the two have silently diverged. | Minor, and observability-only — no request is served differently. The cost lands on the numbers an operator reads: `linkctrl_http_requests_total{surface="redirect"}` and `linkctrl_http_request_duration_seconds{surface="redirect"}` mix short-link traffic with dashboard page loads whose budget is 250ms rather than 20ms, so the redirect surface's own figures are inflated by whatever the dashboard is doing. The SLO itself is unaffected — that is measured by `linkctrl_redirect_duration_seconds`, which the redirect handler records directly and which this does not touch — so the claim in docs/slo.md stands. Not fixed here because adding `/feeds` alone would make the list wrong in a new way, and the honest fix is to derive the classifier from `dashboardPatterns` rather than to extend a second hand-written copy of it, which is a change to a shipped metric's labels. | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. **Remedy pointer corrected 2026-08-04, F85's fix:** this row's evidence names `dashboardPatterns` in `internal/httpx/router.go` as "the list that *is* maintained" and proposes deriving the classifier from it. That slice no longer exists — F85 deleted it, because it had fallen behind the registrations it sat beside. The source this row asks for is now `(*appMux).mounts()`, which is derived from what the application mux was actually handed rather than hand-written, so it is a better one than the row could have had. The finding itself is unchanged and still open. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. The classifier is **derived, not extended**. `observability.SetWebPaths` is called at boot with `(*appMux).mounts()` — what the application mux was actually handed — so the dashboard set cannot fall behind the routes again. The row proposed deriving it from `dashboardPatterns`, which F85 had since deleted for having fallen behind in exactly this way; `mounts()` is the better source it could not have known about, and its own Reviewed cell had already recorded that correction. Adding `/feeds` to the hand-written list would have made the list wrong in a new way, which is why the row refused to do it. `TestEveryDashboardRouteIsCountedAsTheDashboard` drives its assertion from `mounts()` too, so the test cannot drift either — a list checked against a copy of itself is what this finding was. Shown red by removing the boot call: nine routes reported `surface="redirect"`, `/automation` and `/campaigns` and `/disputes` among them. |

Both live callers use the `auth` copy (`internal/audit/audit.go:264`, `internal/auth/service.go:365`). The two functions differ only in comment wording. Found while verifying the 6to4 truncation gap — which is the exact kind of one-line fix that would land in one copy and leave the other passing its own green tests | **Minor.** No current exposure; it is a fix-divergence trap sitting on the privacy boundary. Deleting the dead copy is strictly better than syncing it | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. Deleting rather than syncing is what the row asked for and is the right direction — a fix-divergence trap on the privacy boundary, where the one-line correction lands in one copy and the other keeps passing its own tests. |
| F110 | M44.9 | The audit read API stores, indexes, selects and scans a workspace id it never returns | `internal/audit/audit.go:474-500` (`toEntry`) copies ten fields and not `workspace_id`; `Entry` at `:244-259` declares no such field, and `api/openapi.yaml`'s `AuditEntry` declares none | The write stores it (`audit.go:338-341`), the query selects it (`audit.sql:29`) and the generated row scans it. `audit.sql:25-27` justifies not **filtering** by workspace — *"an audit log that can be narrowed to the workspace the reader happens to be in would hide exactly the actions worth reviewing"* — and says nothing about not returning it, so this is an undocumented second choice riding on a documented first one. Compensation is partial: `workspace.*` actions carry the workspace as `target_id`, but a link-scoped action such as `link.bot_blocking_changed` names only the link | Minor. Read-only reporting gap with no security consequence, and there is no audit UI, so this API response is the entire reader-facing surface. It compounds F31: a workspace-scoped admin already reads the whole organization's rows and cannot tell which came from workspaces they do not administer | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. `workspace_id` is returned by `GET /api/v1/audit` and declared in `api/openapi.yaml`. The schema's description says what the query file already said about *filtering* — a log narrowed to the workspace the reader is standing in would hide exactly the actions worth reviewing — and adds what neither said: returning it is how a reader tells a link-scoped action apart afterwards, since `link.bot_blocking_changed` names only the link where `workspace.*` actions carry the workspace as `target_id` already. The test asserts the organization-level case beside the workspace one, because turning an absent workspace into a zero uuid would read as a workspace nobody can look up; shown red by dropping the field from `toEntry`. |
| F41 | M32.9 | `CacheKeyVersion` was not bumped when M32.5 added bot-policy fields, so a snapshot written by a pre-M32.5 binary reads as "do not block" for up to `REDIRECT_TTL` | `internal/redirect/snapshot.go:44-50` and `internal/redirect/resolver.go:24` | Reproduced at the Redis layer: with `{"block_bots":true}` in Postgres, dropping the in-process copy and overwriting the shared entry with the payload a pre-M32.5 binary would write (identical minus the `omitempty` `bb`/`db`) yielded **three 302s to a bot**, and the app never revalidates or rewrites the entry. Half the finding is dead, though: `SetBotBlocking` calls `InvalidateDomain` unconditionally, which sweeps `lc:a:v1:<domain>:*`, so entries written **before** blocking is switched on have a zero stale window. What survives needs an old binary writing entries **while blocking is already on** — reachable because `docs/releasing.md:102` runs migrations at boot before the listener opens, so a rolling restart has old and new containers serving concurrently, and `kindDomain` is new in `fa0a079`, so an old replica hits `apply`'s `default:` branch and neither clears its tier nor stops writing bot-less entries. `docs/releasing.md:120-124` calls in-series rollback *"safe"* and cross-series residue *"harmless"* | **Low.** Fail-open on an opt-in control whose failure mode is the pre-M32.5 status quo — no disclosure, no authz bypass — but silent, bounded only by a 24h TTL, on a sequence the release documentation calls safe. The comment's justification (*"nobody can have switched blocking on yet"*) is what is actually false | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 7, low. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. **Closed by correcting the reasoning, with no version bump**, and the two halves are separate. The row is right that the comment's justification is false: *"nobody can have switched blocking on yet"* assumes the old build stops before the new one starts, and migrations run at boot before the listener opens, so a rolling restart has both serving at once. But the residue is empty by arithmetic — 0.1.0 is the only release that exists and it keys on `v1`, this build keys on `v3`, and M32.5, M34 and M36 all land inside the same unreleased minor — so no entry any released build wrote is one this build can read. Same shape as [F132](#closed): the argument stays untried, the population is empty. What the comment now carries is the **rule for next time**, which is the part that would otherwise have licensed the same mistake: a new omitempty field may skip the bump only when its zero value means the same thing to a visitor as the true value would, never because nobody has had time to configure it. |
| F65 | M35 | `RedirectHandler.Logger` is dereferenced without a nil check on the redirect path's failure branches, so a handler constructed without one panics on the request it was trying to report | `internal/httpx/redirect.go:206` — `h.Logger.Error("redirect resolution failed", …)`, reached when `Resolver.Resolve` returns an error | Found while wiring M35's gate code, which logs on branches a fixture actually takes. The gate code is nil-tolerant through a `log()` accessor added for this reason (`internal/httpx/redirect_gates.go:52-59`); the pre-existing call above it is not, and neither is the sampled-success line at `:334`. A nil `*slog.Logger` panics inside `slog.(*Logger).Enabled`, which is what the M35 signature test hit before the accessor existed — the connection was closed mid-response and the client saw `EOF`. Production always sets `Logger`, so the reachable case is a fixture or an embedder of `httpx`; the panic is nonetheless on the hot path's 503 branch, i.e. the branch that runs when Postgres is already unwell | Minor. Unreachable in `cmd/linkctrl`, which always sets the field, and every failure branch is one a healthy instance does not take. It is a latent crash in the one place that must degrade rather than fail, and the fix is three lines — use the accessor that now exists | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 6, minor — wrong behaviour nothing can provoke. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. Both raw dereferences now go through the nil-tolerant `log()` accessor M35 added for exactly this reason and then did not retrofit — `redirect.go:230`'s 503 branch and the sampled success line at `:414`. Held by `TestNothingOnTheRedirectPathDereferencesLoggerDirectly`, a scan of the package's `redirect*.go` for `.Logger.<method>(…)`, because the accessor on its own is what already existed and was already being gone round; what makes it hold is that nothing may bypass it. Shown red by restoring one dereference. Production always sets `Logger`, so this is reachable only from a fixture or an embedder — and the branch it lands on is the one that runs when Postgres is already unwell, which is why a latent panic there is worth a guard rather than a comment. |
| F9 | M26.6 | An uncached redirect pays `REDIS_READ_TIMEOUT` twice while Redis is stalled, not once | `internal/redirect/resolver.go` — `fromRedis` (the failed `Get`) and `store` (the `Set` that repopulates), both reached synchronously from `Resolve` | Measured for M26.6 on 2026-07-31, against `redisProxy.blackholed(true)` with a client built the way `internal/platform/redis.Open` builds one (`DialTimeout` 1s, `ReadTimeout` 50ms). A cold `Resolve` took **108ms**; the two following resolves took 0s, because the in-process tier was populated. The 108ms is 50ms of failed lookup, plus the Postgres query, plus 50ms of `Set` against a server that will not answer it either. Both calls are bounded and neither retries, so this is not the compounding M26.6 was scheduled for — that was the invalidation path's three-attempt loop, now bounded — but the `redis.go` comment claiming a stalled Redis "costs a little latency" was written about one call and there are two. | Minor. Nothing is wrong, only slow, and only while Redis is stalled: the redirect is still served from Postgres. The documented uncached target is 100ms and a stalled cache puts a cold redirect just past it, so the cost is an SLO the operator cannot meet during a Redis stall rather than a failure. The obvious fix — skip the repopulating `Set` when the lookup just timed out — is a change to the redirect path and therefore owes the k6 re-run, which is why it is not taken inside a milestone that deliberately left that path alone. | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 4, minor — redirect path and cache. The batch owes one k6 re-run, not six. Deliberately **not merged** with [F98](#closed) by M45's queue hygiene, 2026-08-04. Same class — a stalled Redis costing `REDIS_READ_TIMEOUT` on the redirect path, against a document that says it costs nothing — but different call sites (`resolver.go`'s failed `Get` and repopulating `Set` versus `analytics/returning.go`'s `SISMEMBER`), different cache tiers, different SLOs (100ms uncached, 20ms cached), different false sentences, and incompatible fixes: this row's is to skip the repopulating `Set`, and F98's own note rules out both of the obvious ones. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. The repopulating `Set` is skipped when the lookup that led to it just failed for any reason other than the key being absent — a server that will not answer a `Get` will not usefully answer the `Set`. Recorded on the resolver rather than threaded through the call, because the stall is a property of the server and the write happens inside a singleflight whose leader may be a different request than the one that saw it; the window is `DBTimeout`, one uncached resolve, which is exactly the span between the failed read and the write it provokes. **Deliberately not a circuit breaker**: reads are never skipped, because a read is how the cache recovers. The in-process tier is still populated, so skipping costs only that the shared tier stays cold for a window in which it was cold anyway. `TestAStalledRedisIsPaidForOncePerResolve` runs against a listener that accepts and never answers, at a 400ms timeout so the two outcomes are hundreds of milliseconds apart rather than tens; shown red by removing the skip, at *400.869753ms*. A second test asserts the suppression lapses, so one transient stall does not stop the shared tier being written to for the life of the process. |
| F98 | M44.9 | A cached redirect blocks on Redis for a returning-visitor rule, while two operator documents say a memory-answered redirect costs nothing | `internal/httpx/redirect_rules.go:188-198` → `internal/analytics/returning.go:150-154`, reached from `redirect.go:323` on every `OutcomeRedirect` regardless of cache tier | `internal/platform/redis/redis.go:52-54` and `docs/configuration.md:151-153` both state that *"A redirect already answered from memory costs nothing"*. Since M34 that is false for a link carrying a `returning` condition: the `SISMEMBER` runs on a memory hit, and with Redis stalled it costs `REDIS_READ_TIMEOUT` (50ms) on the path the 20ms SLO is stated over. The framing that the SLO was never measured on this path is **refuted** — `docs/slo.md:230-243` records all 240,002 cache-hit requests performing the lookup and matching, at 100% under 20ms — so what is wrong is the stall case and the two sentences, not the measurement | Minor, and the same class and tier as F9, which covers only the two uncached calls and predates M34. Fix note: making it asynchronous is wrong, because the answer decides which destination is served; skipping the lookup after a Redis failure silently stops a returning-visitor rule matching, which is a behaviour change to a shipped rule rather than a latency fix | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 4, minor — redirect path and cache. The batch owes one k6 re-run, not six. Deliberately **not merged** with [F9](#closed) by M45's queue hygiene, 2026-08-04: same class and tier, different call site, different cache tier, different SLO, and the two fix notes are incompatible. See F9's Reviewed cell for the full comparison. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. **Closed as the two sentences, not as code**, which is what the row's own fix note requires: making the `SISMEMBER` asynchronous answers after the destination has been chosen, and skipping it on a Redis failure silently stops a shipped routing rule matching. Both false sentences are corrected — `internal/platform/redis/redis.go` and `docs/configuration.md:151` each said *"A redirect already answered from memory costs nothing"* — and each now names the exception, scopes it to links carrying a `returning` condition, and says why it cannot be deferred. `docs/slo.md` needed nothing: its measurement already records all 240,002 cache-hit requests performing the lookup, which is why the row scoped itself to the stall case and the sentences rather than to the number. |
| F48 | M32.9 | The root-redirect cache refills on the **application** pool with no timeout and no singleflight, from a mux to which no `RequestTimeout` is applied | `internal/httpx/rootredirect.go:57-71`, with `cmd/linkctrl/main.go:468`/`:506` and `internal/httpx/router.go:438`/`:522` | All three absences verified. Bounded, which changes the emphasis: the path exists **only on split-host deployments** (`registerRoot` is called solely inside the `SplitHosts()` branch), the query is a unique-partial-index lookup on a table holding one row, and `WriteTimeout` (30s) does bound it. So the singleflight half is near-costless — a few duplicate index lookups per TTL, not a herd. The half that matters is the pool crossing: `main.go:278-280` says the redirect pool exists *"so a slow analytics query on the application pool cannot leave a redirect waiting to acquire a connection"*, and this is a redirect-tree path acquiring from `pools.App`. M23 makes the miss fleet-simultaneous, since `Subscriber.flush()` clears every replica's root cache on every re-establishment including the first | **Minor.** A stated architectural guarantee with a real hole, biting only on split-host under app-pool exhaustion, blast radius one URL rather than the alias traffic carrying the SLO | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 4, minor — redirect path and cache. The batch owes one k6 re-run, not six. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. All three absences, and the pool crossing the row calls the half that matters is the one that took a new function. `link.LoadRootRedirectWith` takes a queries handle so the caller chooses the pool, and `main.go` now builds the loader on `pools.Redirect` — the read is two plain statements with no transaction and no service state, so the handle was the whole dependency and the crossing existed only because the sole loader available was a method on an application-pool service. The refill is serialized behind its own mutex, separate from the one guarding the fields so a load cannot block cache *hits*, with a re-check inside; and it carries `DefaultRootLoadTimeout`, because this handler is on the redirect mux and `RequestTimeout` lives in `appHandler`. `TestTheRootRedirectRefillsOnceAndUnderADeadline` was shown red by removing both: *8 concurrent requests produced 8 loads*, and *the refill ran with no deadline*. A second test asserts a lapsed TTL still refills, so serializing did not quietly become a one-shot. |
| F101 | M44.9 | One Redis call on the redirect path carries no deadline, falsifying the premise on which `MaxRetries` was left unpinned | `internal/redirect/resolver.go:257` — `_ = r.redis.Del(context.WithoutCancel(rctx), k).Err()` | `context.WithoutCancel` strips the deadline, not only cancellation — confirmed against the tree's toolchain. D26 and `internal/platform/redis/redis.go:58-61` both justify leaving go-redis's retry count at its default on the stated ground that *"every Redis call site in this tree carries one"*. Decisive beyond the original report: `resolver.go:407`, `:512` and `:590` all use `WithTimeout(WithoutCancel(ctx), …)`, so `:257` is the single site missing the re-wrap — an omission rather than a design | Minor and latent: the branch needs a `Get` that succeeds followed by a decode failure, which requires a snapshot format break — but then it fires on every key rather than one. Fix note: passing `rctx` re-introduces the cancellation `WithoutCancel` is there to strip, so a hung-up client would skip the cleanup and leave the poison key costing a decode per request; and the fix touches the redirect path, which owes a k6 re-run | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 4, minor — redirect path and cache. Held by `TestNoDetachedContextIsHandedStraightToACall` rather than by a unit test of the branch, which needs a snapshot format break to reach: the scan is the durable form, since the row's own evidence is that this was the single site where a rule every neighbour followed had lapsed. |

One unbounded goroutine per verified-pending domain, on an endpoint the deployment guidance tells operators to keep off the internet. The batch owes one k6 re-run, not six. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. `DefaultActivateTimeout`, five seconds, wrapped around the detached context before `Activate` sees it. | F13 | M29 | Registration answers whether an address already has an account, which on an open instance is a public enumeration oracle | `internal/signup/signup.go` — `Register`'s `CountUsersByEmail` check, surfaced as `409` by `WriteError`'s `auth.ErrEmailTaken` case in `internal/httpx/problem.go:137` and as "That email address is already registered." by `SignupSubmit` | Reachable without any credential once the effective mode is `open`: `POST /api/v1/auth/register` with a known address answers `409 conflict`, an unknown one answers `202 verification_sent`, and the browser form makes the same distinction in words. Rate limiting slows a sweep but does not remove the signal. The behaviour is not new — the endpoint has answered `409` for a taken address since Phase 1 — but until M29 the endpoint was API-only and there was no browser form; opening self-serve signup is what makes it a surface a stranger reaches. M27 deliberately spent argon2 work to stop redemption answering the same question (D27), so the product already treats this as something worth not disclosing, and the two surfaces now disagree. | Minor, and bounded by the mode: on a `closed` or `invite` instance the endpoint refuses before it looks anything up, and the shipped default is `closed`. On a deliberately open instance it lets somebody test a leaked address list for membership. The honest fix is to answer `202` either way and mail "you already have an account" to a taken address, which is a behaviour change to a documented status code and a second mail template — scope, not a detail, which is why it is deferred rather than taken inside M29. | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 3, minor — a principal can act on it, in scope by the 2026-08-04 standing rule. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. A taken address now gets the same `202`, the same body and the same argon2 cost as a free one, and the answer goes to the address by mail — `account-exists`, a fourth template — rather than to whoever typed it into the form. The hash is computed **before** the lookup, deliberately: argon2 is this request by two orders of magnitude, so branching first would have left the timing carrying what the status code stopped carrying. Nothing is written for a taken address, which also closes a smaller thing the row did not name — a pending registration would have superseded whatever the real owner had outstanding, so a stranger could invalidate their link by typing their address in. `TestRegistrationCannotBeAskedWhetherAnAddressIsTaken` compares whole response bodies with only the echoed address and the clock normalized out, **and that is what caught the residue**: the taken branch returned Go's nanosecond clock where the free one returned a value Postgres had truncated to a microsecond, so the fractional digits answered the question. Truncated to match. The 409 is gone from `api/openapi.yaml`, and the unreachable branch in `web_signup.go` is deleted rather than left asserting the opposite of the behaviour — its text also offered a password reset this product has never had ([F141](#closed)). |
| F53 | M32.9 | An address that passes `auth.ValidateEmail` but fails `net/mail.ParseAddress` commits a `pending_registrations` row and then answers HTTP 500 — a status the spec does not declare | `internal/signup/signup.go:279-293` via `internal/mail/mail.go:119-124` | Divergence measured: `a<b@c.de`, `a,b@c.de`, `user@exa(mple.com` and six more pass the regex and fail `ParseAddress`. The transaction commits *before* the enqueue, and neither `WriteError` nor `webError` maps the wrapped error, so both fall to `default` → 500 plus an Error-level log per attempt. `api/openapi.yaml:146-164` declares 202/403/409/422/429 and no 5xx. Full precondition: `SIGNUP_MODE=open` **and** a configured mailer — on a default instance `Register` refuses with 403 before validating. The orphan row is not a leak: it expires after `VerificationTTL` (24h) and the hourly `signup-purge` removes it | **Minor.** A genuine contract break plus log noise, self-limited to the attacker's own malformed input. Clean fix is to run the address through `ParseAddress` before the transaction commits, or map the enqueue failure to 422 | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 6, minor — wrong behaviour nothing can provoke. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. Fixed in `auth.ValidateEmail` rather than in `signup.Register`, which is wider than the row asked for and is the same defect: the validator is the shared gate on every path that writes an address, so invitations reached the identical enqueue and the identical 500 through a different door. `net/mail.ParseAddress` is the parser the mailer itself uses, and the check also requires the parse to return exactly the address given, so the display-name form the pattern already rejects cannot arrive by the new route. Strictly a narrowing — nothing the tree accepted before is refused now except the unsendable. `TestAnUnsendableAddressIsRefusedRatherThanCommitted` was shown red by disabling the check: four addresses answered 500 and four rows committed. |
| F103 | M44.9 | `GET /api/v1/workspaces` answers an API key with every organization its owner belongs to | `internal/auth/workspace.go:102` calls `ListWorkspacesForUser(actor.UserID)`; `internal/store/query/workspaces.sql:26-31` filters `m.user_id = $1` with no organization predicate; the route is `RequireAuth` with no permission | Returns id, name, slug, organization id, organization name and personal flag for every workspace the owner belongs to, in any organization. M44 spent an `organization_id` parameter (`auth.sql:145-153`) specifically to stop a key **acting** in a tenant it was not issued for — *"The key would then act in a tenant it was never issued for"* — and the same bound is absent from this read. `workspace.go:95-97` concedes there is no permission *"because it exposes nothing but the caller's own memberships"*, which is true of a person and not of a credential | Minor. Metadata only, no ability to act there, and the gap is credential-versus-person rather than tenant-versus-tenant. Predates M44 — it arrived with M25 and org-wide keys are not required to reach it. Fix note: adding the predicate to the shared query breaks the browser switcher, whose entire job is crossing organizations; the filter belongs in the service on `actor.IsAPIKey()`, which collides with the rule F104 is about | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 3, minor — a principal can act on it, in scope by the 2026-08-04 standing rule. | **[M45](phase-details/m45.md)**, 2026-08-05. `Service.Workspaces` now drops any row whose organization is not the key's own. The filter is in the service and **not** in `ListWorkspacesForUser`, exactly as this row's fix note required: that query also serves the browser switcher, whose entire job is to cross organizations, so a predicate there would have closed the disclosure by breaking the feature. `TestAKeyIsToldOnlyAboutItsOwnOrganization` asserts both halves in one fixture — the owner's session is still offered the second organization's workspace, the key issued before that organization existed is not — and it was shown red by disabling the filter, which reported *saw map[Default:true Their Space:true]*. Closed together with [F104](#closed) because the fix is an eleventh site branching on credential type, and F104 is the rule that said nothing may. **Amended 2026-08-08 by [M54](phase-details/m54.md), not reopened.** The bound described here stands, unchanged, for a key **pinned** to one organization — which is every key issued before 0.3.0 and every key whose creator names an organization. It lapses for an *account-wide* key, because the premise this row rests on is that a key is issued for one organization, and that stopped being true of every key: an account-wide key acts in the organizations its owner belongs to, so the tenants it would be reading about are the tenants it works in, and bounding it to whichever one the current request resolved into would hide the only surface that says where else it reaches. `Identity.APIKeyOrgID` is what lets `Service.Workspaces` tell the two apart — nil for a session and for an account-wide key, set for a pinned one — so the eleventh credential-type branch F104 admitted is still one branch and not two. |

What is genuinely false is `requireSessionActor`'s own comment at `internal/auth/apikey.go:682-685` — *"`apikeys.*` is already non-delegable, so no key holds the permission these operations check"* — which holds for its three callers in that file and is false for `workspace.go:135` and `:178`, which check no permission at all, where the function is the only enforcement rather than defence in depth | Minor, latent, and it lands on the next milestone that reads the rule literally and removes a session gate it does not understand. Fix note: deleting `requireSessionActor` from `workspace.go` to satisfy the rule hands a leaked key the ability to move its owner's browser — and, since M44, its own landing workspace. Fix the sentence, not the calls | **Approved as work 2026-08-05**, M45's third triage — Tier 5, minor — one-edit corrections. The rule in [phase-details/README.md](phase-details/README.md) is amended, owner-answered, from two mechanisms to four. **The census corrected the row's count**: ten sites branched on credential type where this row said seven, and four of the four it missed — `team.go:234`, `team.go:285`, `invite.go:336`, `invite.go:524` — are D43's cap, which the rule already licensed but described as covering invitations when the tree also caps role assignment against an existing membership.
