| F132 | M30 | A destination stored by an earlier build keeps the spelling it was stored with, so a link created before the UTS-46 fold still holds and still serves a host that the tiers would now refuse | `internal/link/destination.go` — `canonicalHost` runs inside `ValidateDestination`, which is reached only from `Service.checkDestination`, i.e. only when a destination is *written*; nothing reads the tiers on the redirect path | F77 was reproduced live against the 0.1.x build, so any instance that was probed holds rows whose `url` is a percent-encoded non-ASCII host — `http://169%E3%80%82254%E3%80%82169%E3%80%82254/` and the like — and the redirect serves that value verbatim. The fix converts on write only; editing the link converts it, and nothing sweeps. Same shape as F26's residue, which was never swept either | **Low.** Re-checking already-accepted links against new tiers is *Not in Phase 2* in Plan.md and predates both reopenings, so this is the general limitation with a specific instance rather than a new gap. It is filed because the *specific* instance is a stored open redirect at a metadata endpoint rather than a stale blocklist match, and because nothing else in the tree records that closing F77 left live rows behind **Corrected 2026-08-05 (M45): the second reason was false when this row was filed, and the row's own commit is what falsifies it.** `Plan.md`'s *A destination stored by an earlier build…* limitation row (`:846` today) has recorded that closing F77 left live rows behind since `98cc6de` — *"There is no sweep, and building one is the separate job that row names"* — and `98cc6de` is not merely F77's closing commit, it is the commit that filed **this row**: `git log -S` finds both strings entering the tree in that one diff, and neither has moved since. So the clause claiming nothing else in the tree records the residue was contradicted by a line added beside it. **The first reason stands**, and is the whole of what this row is worth: that row said *"including one that would now be refused"* without saying that the refused thing is the cloud metadata endpoint. **The residue was also wider than this cell and the Finding cell say.** [F26](#closed)'s trailing-dot trim runs on write only for exactly the reason [D93](decisions.md)'s mapping does — both live inside `canonicalHost` — so the class covers `169.254.169.254.` as well as `169。254。169。254`, and `git show v0.1.0:internal/link/destination.go` confirms it: no `TrimRight` on the host anywhere, and `looksNumeric` answering false on an empty last label. That row now names both folds and both spellings. | Reviewed 2026-08-05 by the owner, who supplied the fact that decides it: **no server has ever been built using 0.1.0.** Declined rather than approved as work — the third disposition [F114](#closed) established. | **M45, recorded and declined** — [F114](#closed)'s third disposition, for a different reason: not that every repair costs more than the defect, but that **the population a repair would act on is empty**. A one-time `lctl` command that folded stored hosts and quarantined what no longer passed was the shape considered, and 0.2.0 being the first release of this product ever deployed is what disqualifies it — every instance in existence begins with both folds already in place and holds no pre-fold spelling to convert, so the command would have been an operator surface for zero rows, with the power to archive a tenant's link on an instance whose operator never asked for it. The `v0.1.0` tag and its release are public — `88de5ee`, published 2026-07-31 — so this is not a claim that nobody anywhere can hold such a row; it is that holding one takes an instance created before the fold **and** then given such a host, and no instance answering that description is accountable to this project. **What is built instead is the record.** `Plan.md`'s limitation row stated the UTS-46 spelling alone and now states the residue precisely: both folds, both spellings, the metadata endpoint by name, and the open-redirect-rather-than-SSRF distinction this product's own inability to fetch a destination buys. The general job — re-checking already-accepted links against new tiers — stays *Not in Phase 2* at `Plan.md:197` and `:740`, one job with one decision, which is what a narrower predicate shipped at phase close would have quietly split in two. See decisions.md. |
| F140 | M45 | An instance whose principal loses their account has no in-product way to appoint another one, and the operator's only route is `psql` | `internal/auth/instance.go` — `InstanceGrantable` holds `destinations.review` and `destinations.decide` and deliberately not `instance.admin`; `internal/instance/instance.go` `RevokeReviewer` loops over that same set, so no surface confers or withdraws the principal; `internal/auth/service.go` confers it only under `IsFirstUser`, and `NeedsSetup` is false forever once one account exists | Named while building D98 rather than found by testing, and bounded before it was written: **nothing in this product deletes or soft-deletes a `users` row** — no `DELETE FROM users` and no write to `users.deleted_at` anywhere in `internal/store/query/`, checked 2026-08-04 — so a conferred principal cannot be lost through the product. What is reachable is an operator losing access to the account itself: a forgotten password with no mailer configured, a departed colleague, or an instance whose earliest surviving account was never theirs, which migration 03400 confers on. `docs/operations.md` documents the `INSERT INTO instance_grants` that fixes it | **Minor**, and arguably not a defect: the fix needs the database, and the operator has the database while the person who lost the account does not, which is the same trust basis `/setup` rests on. It is recorded because the *obvious* repair is the one D98 forbids — an operation that confers `instance.admin` would let a principal mint another principal, and the delegation bound exists precisely to stop the set of delegators growing. Any in-product recovery path is a variation on that, so this needs a decision rather than a fix. A CLI command, `lctl` acting on the same filesystem-access trust `/setup` claims, is the shape most likely to be right | Approved as work 2026-08-05 — the owner's review of the rows M45's own fix work produced, **and the owner chose the shape: an `lctl` command**, which is what this row's severity cell nominates. [F141](#closed), filed the day before, is why it is worth more than *Minor*: there is no account recovery in this product, so the principal's password and the principal are one thing to lose. Fixed under **M45's own number**. | **M45**, `internal/instance` and a new `cmd/lctl/instance.go`. `lctl instance principal show` names who administers the box; `lctl instance principal move --to <email>` hands it over. **It moves and cannot add**: the transaction confers all four `InstancePrincipalScopes` on the target, withdraws all four from every other holder, and re-reads the holders of `instance.admin` before committing — anything other than exactly the named account refuses and changes nothing. That check is [D98](decisions.md)'s delegation bound asserted rather than claimed, and it is why the recovery does not undermine the thing it recovers. `Service.MovePrincipal` takes **no actor and makes no permission check**, because the authority is filesystem access to the box — the claim `internal/auth`'s `Register` already rests the whole setup flow on — and asking for a `--user` to act as would be asking the operator which identity to pretend to be. The absence of a check is safe only while nothing routes there, so `TestThePrincipalMoveIsReachableFromTheShellAndNowhereElse` scans the tree and fails the build on the symbol appearing outside `internal/instance` and `cmd/lctl`. It is guarded like `lctl seed` and `lctl demo` — refused under `APP_ENV=production` without `--force` — and audited instance-wide with the actor recorded as `system`, since nobody signed in to do it. **No migration**: the four queries it needs already existed. | F138 | M45 | A context deadline handed to any Redis call in this tree is inert, because the client never enables the option that would make go-redis honour it | `internal/platform/redis/redis.go:28-68` — `Open` sets `DialTimeout`, `ReadTimeout`, `WriteTimeout`, `PoolSize` and `MinIdleConns`, and never `ContextTimeoutEnabled` — against `baseClient.context` at `github.com/redis/go-redis/v9@v9.21.0/redis.go:1033-1038`, which hands the socket deadline `context.Background()` unless that option is set | Measured at the pinned version while correcting [F8](#closed), against a listener that accepts and never answers: a command carrying a **50ms** context cost **401ms** with `ReadTimeout` 400ms, on a connection established beforehand and then left to go quiet as well as on one still being made. The same probe with `ContextTimeoutEnabled: true` failed in **50ms**. So the option is what the tree hand-builds twice: `internal/ratelimit/shared.go:164-210` abandons the call to a goroutine and enforces the deadline with a timer, and D26's `REDIS_INVALIDATE_BUDGET` bounds the retry loop that turned the same property into 9.07s ([F2](#closed)). `redis.go:39-49` already says the resolver's per-lookup deadline is not what holds it, so the fact is recorded — what is not recorded anywhere is that a one-line option would make every context bound its own call | Minor, and latent rather than active: every path that matters is bounded by something else, and both bounds are documented. Filed by the comment sweep that re-measured [F8](#closed)'s mechanism rather than inheriting it. | **M45**, `internal/platform/redis/redis.go` — `opt.ContextTimeoutEnabled = true`, and nothing else changes. Every call site was walked before it was set. It can only **shorten** a call: `pool.Conn.deadline` takes the minimum of `now+ReadTimeout` and the context's deadline, so `REDIS_READ_TIMEOUT` is still the ceiling and the two sites that ask for more — the analytics pipeline's 5s, the readiness probe's 750ms — were already 50ms on the wire. Almost every other site asks for exactly the ceiling, because `Options.RedisTimeout` *is* `REDIS_READ_TIMEOUT`. The two that carry no deadline at all still carry none: `fromRedis`'s corrective `Del` and `sweepRedis`'s walk both go through `context.WithoutCancel`, which drops the deadline with the cancellation. So the one behaviour that changes is a caller with **less** than the ceiling left, which on the redirect path is the request context. The redirect path's k6 measurement was re-run; see `docs/slo.md`. |
| F114 | M44.9 | Filing a dispute answers whether an open dispute already exists for any host | `internal/dispute/dispute.go:323-329` maps the unique violation to a conflict returned to the filer; `internal/httpx/web_disputes.go:128-129` renders it as a distinct outcome; `01600_destination_disputes.sql:81-82` carries no tenancy column | Combined with F95's observation that `url_credentials` blocks any host, the probe works for arbitrary targets with only `links.create`, needing no `destinations.review`. Narrowed by verification: **the oracle destroys itself** — a negative probe *creates* the open dispute it was testing for, notifies every owner, and leaves a queue row carrying the filer's identity, so each host answers truthfully exactly once and never quietly. **Re-derived 2026-08-04 (M45), after F33's repair landed:** the oracle survives and its *key* moved. `03300` adds a second partial unique index on `destination_disputes.blocked_host`, so a filing now also conflicts when an open dispute exists about the **blocklist entry** that refuses the probed host — `login.evil.example` and `mail.evil.example` are one answer where they used to be two. The conflict is still reported to the filer as a distinct outcome, and its wording changed from *that destination* to *that refusal* — which is more accurate about the new bound and no less an answer | Low. Leaks existence rather than content — no filer identity, no attempted URL, no organization — and F15 already grants every owner the full queue. Loud, one-shot per host, self-poisoning. Fix note: collapsing the conflict into the success answer breaks the honest case, where a second filer needs to be told their appeal is already queued; adding a tenancy column to the index removes the bound `01600:67-73` claims and the instance-wide semantics the service argues for, so it is a tenancy decision rather than a de-duplication tweak. **Re-derived 2026-08-04 (M45) a second time, against the mitigation the owner asked to have evaluated.** One fact in this cell moved and the verdict did not: *F15 already grants every owner the full queue* is stale, because `03400_instance_principal.sql:108-109` deletes `destinations.review` from the owner role — but `notify.EveryOwner` was left untouched deliberately, so `internal/dispute/dispute.go:691` still walks every organization and `:703-710` puts the defanged host and the reason code in every owner's inbox on every accepted filing. The mechanism changed and the narrowing survives. What decides every proposed mitigation is that **the two answers do not cost the same**. A *yes* is a failed INSERT at `dispute.go:368-379` — no row, no notification, repeatable, and unmetered, because `internal/httpx/router.go:628` mounts `POST /disputes` behind `signedIn` alone while the limiter at `:770` covers only `APIPrefix`. A *no* writes an attributable row, notifies every owner, and flips the answer to yes for everyone afterwards. So the free, silent, unbounded half is *yes*, and the *no* half is the one this row already calls self-limiting. **A per-filer cap on open disputes was evaluated and is not a mitigation for this row**: it can only ever bound the *no* answers, and its two orderings each fail structurally rather than by tuning N — checked before the INSERT it closes the oracle only for a filer who has chosen to spend the cap on N loud attributable writes, and it breaks the honest second-filer case this cell protects by answering *too many open appeals* to somebody whose appeal genuinely is queued; checked after, the conflict wins and the oracle is untouched. What a cap does bound is disputes-per-account, which is [F137](#closed)'s flood rather than this row's disclosure — and F137 is unapproved, and its own fix note already rejects *capping the filer* because the multiplier is `EveryOwner`. Rate-limiting the browser route fails the same test: it meters the answers without removing one, and it is likewise F137's. **The only change that makes this row's claim false is removing the distinct outcome**, and with both rule-outs above standing that means a co-appellant model — one decision row, many appellants, every filing answered identically because it genuinely is queued for all of them. Sized and rejected as worse than the finding: `internal/httpx/api_disputes.go:53-58` returns the whole `Dispute` to the filer, so the obvious implementation hands a prober the first filer's address out of `filed_by`, a **wider** disclosure than the existence bit being closed; avoiding that needs the appellant row to carry its own id, created_at and label so `File` can synthesize an indistinguishable reply, plus `tellFiler` fanning out to N appellants, which newly builds a filer-controlled fan-out on the decide path — the shape F137 complains about. That is a migration, three queries, a restructured `File` and `tellFiler`, a queue change and an API semantic change, at phase close, against a Low existence-only leak | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), with F33 and F95. Deliberately **not merged** into [F33](#closed) by M45's queue hygiene, 2026-08-04, although F95 was: this is a disclosure defect rather than a queue-integrity one, its fix note opens a tenancy question F33's repair does not, and the honest second-filer case it protects has no counterpart there. The `url_credentials` observation it leans on now lives in F33's evidence, folded in with F95. Reviewed 2026-08-04 on M45's triage: **approved as work**, then examined against the tree once F33 had landed and **deliberately not built**. | **M45, approved and declined** — the third disposition, and the Closed section's intro already allows for a row that leaves without being fixed. The oracle's two answers do not cost the same: *yes* is a failed insert, free, silent and unmetered on the browser route, while *no* writes an attributable row and notifies every organization owner. Every mitigation proposed for this row bounds the second. A per-filer cap checked before the insert closes the oracle only for a filer who chose to spend it, and refuses a second filer whose appeal genuinely is queued; checked after, the conflict wins and nothing changes — what it really bounds is disputes per account, which is [F137](#closed)'s flood, unapproved and already rejecting this repair on its own row. The one change that removes the distinct outcome is a co-appellant model whose obvious implementation returns the first filer's address to whoever probed. Recorded in decisions.md and in commit `90c51ec` so the argument survives rather than the conclusion alone. |
| F15 | M31 | Every organization owner on an instance can read every dispute filed on it, and lift a blocklist entry for everybody | `internal/store/migrations/01600_destination_disputes.sql` — the grant of `destinations.review` to the owner role; `internal/dispute/dispute.go` — `List` and `decide`, both scoped by the permission and by nothing else; `internal/store/query/disputes.sql` — `ListDestinationDisputes`, which carries no organization predicate | Built into M31 by the shape of what it governs, and confirmed by `TestTheQueueIsGatedOnTheNewPermission`: the queue query has no `organization_id` filter, and `DeleteBlockedDestination` removes a row from a table [01500](../../internal/store/migrations/01500_destination_blocking.sql) makes instance-wide on purpose. So on an instance with two organizations, A's owner sees that somebody in B tried to link to a given host, sees their address in `filed_by`, and can delete the entry that refuses it for both. With `LINKCTRL_SIGNUP_MODE=open` (M29) that reach is one registration away, because registering provisions an organization and makes the registrant its owner. The queue *cannot* honestly be narrower than the decision — a per-organization view over an instance-wide effect would hide rows the same reader is deciding for — so this is the tenancy model showing through rather than a scoping mistake. | Moderate, and the same shape as something already shipped rather than new: `domains.write` has been granted to owner and admin since 00800 and changes where the link domain's root sends every workspace's stray visitors. This is one degree wider because it decides what every workspace may link to, and because M31 adds a cross-organization *read* that `domains.write` does not have. The root cause is D38's finding that this product has no instance-level principal, so "the instance owner" is not a thing the permission system can name; every fix — an instance-owner flag, an environment-named moderator, or scoping the blocklist per organization and giving up 01500's reasoning — is a design decision rather than a correction. Bounded meanwhile by the shipped signup default (`closed`) and by admins deliberately not holding the permission. | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), which took F15, F31 and F36 together and recorded that the shape was blocked on a design fork the owner had not been asked. That fork is [D98](decisions.md). | **M45**, 2026-08-04 (`docs/build-notes/decisions.md`, *M45, building the principal D98 authorized*). Closed by **D98**, the owner's choice among the four shapes this row's severity column enumerates: the instance-level principal D38 said did not exist. `03400` adds `instance_grants` — a permission held by a **person** over the whole instance, keyed on `user_id` rather than on an organization, because binding instance-wide authority to a founding organization is what D38 refused. The migration **removes** `destinations.review` from the owner role and confers the four instance scopes on the earliest surviving account, which on any instance that went through `/auth/setup` is the setup account; the direction only ever narrows, from a set that grows with every registration to one person. `Register(IsFirstUser)` confers the same four in the transaction that claims a fresh instance, and ordinary registration confers nothing — which is the half that would rebuild this row in one line. The permission **splits**: `destinations.review` reads the queue and **leaves** `NonDelegableScopes`, `destinations.decide` acts on one and enters it, so *"read it with a token, change it with a person"* is the map rather than a branch on credential type the inherited rule forbids. The principal may appoint reviewers (`/disputes`, `POST /api/v1/instance/reviewers`) and **a reviewer may not**: `instance.admin` is not in `auth.InstanceGrantable`, so no grant produces another grantor. What this row records as the *reach* is real and unchanged in kind — that account and its appointees read every dispute on the instance — and the Plan.md limitation row says so. Deliberately untouched: `domains.write` ([F70](#closed)), and `notify.EveryOwner`, so a filing still notifies every organization owner ([F49](#closed), [F137](#closed)). Six sabotages across the four new tests, each red then counter-edited back. |
| F31 | M32.9 | A workspace-scoped admin reads the **entire organization's** audit log, including workspaces where they hold no membership at all | `internal/store/query/audit.sql:33` (`WHERE organization_id = @organization_id`) and `internal/audit/audit.go:313` (`org := actor.OrgID`) | Verified against the live migrated database inside `BEGIN … ROLLBACK`: a user whose only membership is `role=admin, workspace_id=A` resolves `audit.read` and `members.write` in A and **0 permissions** in C, yet `ListAuditLogs` scoped to the organization returns C's rows — a planted `member.added` carrying `{"email":"carol@example.com"}` and a `destination.blocked` carrying `url_defanged`, each with `ip_prefix` and `actor_label`. The stronger form also holds: after the org-wide membership is removed she holds zero permissions in B and C, still resolves into A, and still reads their rows. The verifier corrected the severity argument — member emails are *already* org-wide readable by design, so what is new is `ip_prefix` bound to a named actor, the per-action timeline, and `url_defanged`, for workspaces they do not administer. `audit.sql:26-28` argues the org scope deliberately, but that reasoning is M21's, from when `audit.read` could only be held organization-wide; D31 enumerates the union's costs and does not name this one | **Moderate.** An unexamined composition of two intended decisions rather than an accepted trade-off. Read-only, leaves no trace (reads are not audited), and needs a direct JSON call since no UI page exists. Same root as F27 — the fix for one may not fix the other, since this is a read scope and F27 is a write scope | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), which took F15, F31 and F36 together and recorded that the shape was blocked on a design fork the owner had not been asked. This row's diagnosis is exactly right and the repair is the rule it names arriving one axis over: **D44** already says authority is per scope and only an organization-wide membership reaches the organization-wide scope, and until now that governed writes only. `auth.MembershipAuthority.Scopes()` answers which scopes a permission is held in; `audit.Service.List` loads it and `ListAuditLogs` gains `org_wide OR workspace_id = ANY(...)`. An organization-wide reader is **unaffected** — `audit.sql:26-28`'s argument is M21's and still correct — and a workspace-scoped one reads the workspaces its own scope covers. Rows with a NULL `workspace_id` are organization-level acts and a workspace-scoped reader does not see them, because `= ANY` is false against NULL, which is the same asymmetry `In(nil)` enforces for writes. `Can` stays the gate for *may you read at all*; the authority bounds *which rows*. The `workspace_ids` parameter is never nil — a nil `uuid[]` reaches Postgres as NULL and `= ANY(NULL)` is unknown, which would return nothing — even though `Can` makes the empty case unreachable. Fixed **on its own terms** rather than through the principal: F31 is an organization's log read by one of its own members, and the instance surface D98 adds is a different question, which is [F36](#closed). Its sibling [F27](#closed) was a write scope and closed separately, as this row predicted. `TestAWorkspaceScopedAdminReadsOnlyTheirOwnWorkspacesAuditRows` builds the row's own fixture — one workspace-scoped admin membership, no organization-wide one — and its sabotage forces `orgWide` true, which reproduces the finding verbatim. |
| F36 | M32.9 | An instance-wide administrative act is recorded in exactly one tenant's audit log and is invisible to every tenant it changed | `internal/audit/audit.go:247` (stamps `actor.OrgID`) and `internal/store/query/audit.sql:33` (reads the same) | Verified against live data: the `domains` table holds one row with `organization_id` NULL, and `SetDefaultDomainBotBlocking` / `SetDefaultDomainRootRedirect` both target `WHERE is_default`, so both actions are unambiguously instance-wide. Five `domain.bot_blocking_changed` rows are logged under `Tenant B` and `AdvVerify-…`; the same query run as *Test Owner* — whose links those settings govern, and which has a `link.bot_blocking_changed` row of its own — returns none of them. `block_bots_enforced` was `t` at the time: every link in that organization was being forced to block bots by a change its owner's audit log does not contain. The D38/F15 defence (*there is no instance principal*) does not stick: `audit_logs.organization_id` is **nullable** and the product already uses a NULL org to mean instance-wide for the `domains` row, so writing these with `organization_id = NULL` and widening the read to `… OR organization_id IS NULL` is one predicate and one branch, naming no principal and granting nobody anything | **Moderate**, for accountability rather than confidentiality — nothing leaks in either direction. `docs/SECURITY.md:55` tells the reader *"Both changes are audited"*, and a reader holding `audit.read` acts on that, looks, and finds nothing. Latent until an instance has two organizations. The `dispute.allowed`/`upheld` limb overlaps F15; the domain-settings limb does not | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), which took F15, F31 and F36 together and recorded that the shape was blocked on a design fork the owner had not been asked. That fork is [D98](decisions.md). | **M45**, 2026-08-04 (`docs/build-notes/decisions.md`, *M45, building the principal D98 authorized*). The write half is this row's fix note taken literally: `audit.Event.InstanceWide` leaves `organization_id` and `workspace_id` NULL while recording the actor in full, because what moved is the tenancy and not the accountability. Marked on both limbs the row names — `SetRootRedirect` and `SetBotBlocking`, which target the instance default and every domain row respectively — plus every dispute decision, which deletes from a blocklist every organization is refused by, and the two writes M45 itself adds to the reviewer roster. `recordDomainEvent` is deliberately **not** marked: a registered hostname belongs to an organization. The read half is **not** the widened predicate the row proposes, and the difference is the reason this could not have been closed when it was filed. `... OR organization_id IS NULL` turns `audit_logs_org_time_idx` from a range scan into a bitmap scan and a sort on a table designed to grow forever; and more importantly there was nobody to give the rows to — *"naming no principal and granting nobody anything"* leaves the surface unreadable, which is a deletion rather than a repair. **D98 supplies the principal**, so `ListInstanceAuditLogs` and `GET /api/v1/instance/audit` are their own surface behind `audit.read.instance`, held by the principal alone and not delegable, for `audit.read`'s reason. `docs/SECURITY.md` says where the records went. The `dispute.allowed`/`upheld` limb this row shares with [F15](#closed) is closed by the same commit; the domain-settings limb is closed here. `TestAnInstanceWideActIsRecordedAgainstNoTenant` asserts the record **moved** rather than vanished, and its sabotage restores the actor's tenancy, which reproduces the finding. |
| F8 | M26.6 | The rate limiter's comment blames a stalled Redis on connection establishment, which measurement contradicts | `internal/ratelimit/shared.go:143`–`:144`, in `take`'s doc comment: "does not honour the context deadline go-redis is handed **when it has to establish a connection**" | Measured for M26.6 on 2026-07-31. The context is not honoured on a stalled read whether or not a connection has to be established: a client with `ReadTimeout` 400ms took 401ms to fail a command carrying a 50ms context both against a server that never answered the handshake *and* down a connection established beforehand and then left to go quiet mid-command. What bounds the wait is the client's own `ReadTimeout`; connection establishment has nothing to do with it, and a dial in isolation does honour the deadline (8s dial timeout, 50ms context, 50ms elapsed). The comment's conclusion — enforce the deadline from outside the call — is correct and the code is correct; only the stated reason is wrong, which makes it the kind of comment that misleads the next person deciding whether the same defence is needed somewhere else. | Cosmetic, and comment-only. No behaviour is affected and M24's mechanism is the right one. It is recorded rather than corrected in place because M26.6's bullets reach the invalidation path, the redirect path and F2's row, and none of them reach `internal/ratelimit` — a comment correction there is out of spec, and this repository's rule is that a shipped milestone's claim is reopened by the owner's scheduling, not by the worker who noticed. | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the cheap comment sweep. Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45**, `internal/ratelimit/shared.go` — `take`'s doc keeps its conclusion and loses its reason. Re-measured at the pinned go-redis **v9.21.0** rather than taken from this row: a command carrying a 50ms context against a server that accepts and never answers cost **401ms** with `ReadTimeout` 400ms, on a connection established beforehand and then left to go quiet as well as on one still being made, while a bare dial under the same context cost 50ms. The mechanism turned out to be nameable, which the row could not say: `baseClient.context` (`redis.go:1033-1038`) hands the socket deadline `context.Background()` unless `Options.ContextTimeoutEnabled` is set, and `internal/platform/redis` does not set it — setting it in the probe brought the same stall to 50ms. So the comment now says `ReadTimeout` bounds the wait and points at `internal/platform/redis`, whose own comment has carried the measurement since M26.6. F2's *multiple seconds* is deliberately not carried forward: [F2](#closed)'s own Closed cell corrects that to 215ms through the shipped client. The option this uncovered is [F138](#closed), not a change here. |

Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45**, `cmd/linkctrl/main.go` — the false half of the justification replaced and the placement kept, which is what the row's fix note asked for. `NextVariantRotation` is now named as what it is: a write M36 hung off this service that a **sequential split** performs on every hit, and `Gated()` (`internal/redirect/snapshot.go:378-380`) excludes such a link. | F122 | M44.9 | Three code comments describe an organization-wide API key's reach as something the credential does not have | `internal/auth/apikey.go:557-558`, `internal/auth/workspace.go:130-133`, `docs/cli.md:131` | The **behaviour is licensed and is not a finding**: D90 records that an organization-wide key is *"resolved the same way a login is"* and *"still follows the person it acts as — pinned default included — within its own organization"*, fixing only the tenancy as a bound rather than a precedence rung, and `docs/SECURITY.md:51` and `docs/usage.md:365-368` say the same in product voice. What is false is the reasoning: `apikey.go:557-558` claims *"No session id: a key is not a browser, and a switch made in one must not move a key's requests"* — omitting the session id removes only the first precedence rung, and the switcher's second write lands on `users.last_workspace_id`, which decides for every account that has not pinned a default. `workspace.go:130-133`'s inference inverts for the NULL-workspace key M44 introduced. `cli.md:131`'s *"valid in every workspace of the organization"* is unqualified, and there is no per-request workspace selector anywhere in `internal/httpx` | Cosmetic, documentation and comments only. Filed because two independent readers reached the behaviour and filed it as a high-severity defect before D90 refuted it — which is what a misleading comment costs. Fix note: pinning the key's workspace at issue time reverses D90 and breaks the test that asserts it | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the cheap comment sweep. Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45**, three sites, behaviour untouched. `internal/auth/apikey.go`'s organization-wide branch: passing no session id kills rung 1 of `ResolveWorkspaceForUser`'s precedence (`auth.sql:118-121`) and does nothing else, so the comment no longer claims a switch cannot move the key — `SwitchWorkspace`'s other write is `users.last_workspace_id`, rung 3, which answers for every owner who has not pinned a default. `internal/auth/workspace.go`: the session requirement keeps both of its true reasons — `SetSessionWorkspace` needs a session id a key has not got, and the other write is the person's — and the inference that inverts for a NULL-workspace key is stated as false rather than deleted, because a reader who assumes it is the one this row was filed by. `docs/cli.md`: `--org-wide` no longer reads as *valid in every workspace at once*; it says there is no per-request selector and that each request resolves one workspace the way a sign-in does, bounded to the key's own organization — which is what `docs/SECURITY.md:51` and `docs/usage.md:367-370` already said in product voice. D90 is untouched in both directions. **The three-site list was checked rather than trusted, and it was short.** The *acts in the workspace its own row names* inference is stated at four more places — `internal/httpx/api_workspaces.go:35-38`, `docs/usage.md:1092-1097`, `CHANGELOG.md`'s M25 entry and `api/openapi.yaml:3243` — and all four are corrected here, because a repair that leaves four copies of the sentence it just called false is not a repair. The *valid in every workspace* phrasing survives at five short-form sites and is **[F139](#closed)** instead: it is incomplete rather than false, and one of the five is a published API contract description. |
| F124 | M44.9 | The IPv6 rate-limit key is documented in the code as covering a case it does not | `internal/ratelimit/ratelimit.go:13-14` and `:314-320` say *"A single host is routinely handed a /64 **or larger**"* while `Key` at `:321-332` folds to `/64`; `docs/configuration.md:384` drops the qualifier entirely | A delegated `/48` or `/56` yields many distinct buckets at no cost. Same family as F67 | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the comment sweep. Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45**, `internal/team/organization.go`, comment only. The comment now says what the placement buys (one transaction) and what it does not (serialization); names read committed as the reason a redemption committing after the read is invisible to it, nothing in the tree setting a stronger isolation level; and points at `LockOrganizations`, the `FOR UPDATE` pattern `organizations.sql:6-16` prescribes for exactly this and which this call deliberately does not use. |

One blocked row for `evil.example` produced two open disputes from `login.` and `mail.` children. The dashboard `POST /disputes` (`router.go:373`) carries **no rate limiter at all**. Falsifies `01600_destination_disputes.sql:26-30` in both clauses — *"it is the host the refusal actually matched rather than the one that was typed"* — and its stated bound at `:67-73`. Needs only `links.create`. The decision-integrity half (the string shown is not the string the button acts on) is worth more than the flood. Both halves, and the two things it deliberately is not. `03300` adds `destination_disputes.blocked_host`, written at filing from `link.Verdict.ListedHost` — a new field set in `listedInDatabase` where the match happens and nowhere else, so it cannot name a row that did not refuse — and `entryToLift` now reads it and asks the new `GetBlockedDestination` for that exact host. `MatchBlockedDestination` has one caller again, which is the package that judges destinations. The residue is **F137**, and 01600's index comment now claims only what the index can keep. **F15 is untouched in both directions**: the new bound is instance-wide because the list it argues with is, so answering F15 by scoping disputes per organization would need an organization column on both indexes, not one. **[F114](#closed) is not closed** — its key moved from the typed host to the entry, which reduces the questions the oracle answers and removes none; it returns as an unanswered prompt. Fix note: mapping the error to 401 fixes the API and leaves the browser copy leaking, and a real user then gets "wrong password" during their own lockout with no explanation. Timing is a second leak — the locked branch returns before any verification, so it is fast where the unknown-address path pays a dummy argon2 | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the moderate security cluster (all eleven). Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45** (`docs/build-notes/decisions.md`, *M45, two refusals that answered a question nobody asked them*), and both traps the fix note named are answered rather than half-answered. `ErrAccountLocked` now reaches the API folded into the `invalid-credentials` 401 alongside `ErrAccountInactive`, and the sign-in form renders the *same string*, `SignInRefusedDetail`, that the API's body carries — one constant used by both call sites rather than two wordings that agree today, because mapping the error and leaving the prose is exactly what the note warned about. **The timing half is fixed too**: the locked branch spends `DummyVerify` before returning, so it no longer refuses in one database round trip where every other refusal pays an argon2 hash. The lockout is named **unconditionally** in that string — identical whether or not the address exists and whether or not a lockout is in force, so it answers nothing while still telling the one person entitled to know why waiting helps; the generic `WriteError` detail is deliberately unchanged, because it also serves `POST /api/v1/auth/password` where no lockout exists. `ErrAccountLocked` stays a distinct sentinel: the collapse belongs at the two boundaries that answer a person, and `TestAccountLocksAfterRepeatedFailures` still asserts it. **`account-locked` is removed as a problem type** — `rate-limited` is now the only 429 this API produces — and that client-visible break is in CHANGELOG.md, `api/openapi.yaml`, `docs/usage.md` and `docs/operations.md`, which gains the query an operator needs now that the response will not tell them. Driven by `TestSignInAnswersALockedAccountLikeAnUnregisteredOne` (both surfaces, attempt by attempt through the threshold) and `TestALockedAccountCostsTheSameAsAnUnregisteredOne`, both sabotage-verified. **F13 is not closed by this** and stays open, which is the distinction the severity cell drew. |
| F133 | M42 | The mail outbox has the defect [F82](#closed) named for webhooks, on the same goroutine and one line earlier — a relay that accepts connections and never answers holds every scheduled job on the instance for `SMTP_TIMEOUT` × `DrainBatch` | `internal/mail/mail.go:164-166` sends a claimed batch sequentially; `DrainBatch = 20` at `:62`; `SMTP_TIMEOUT` defaults to `10s` (`internal/config/config.go:339`) and becomes the dial deadline at `internal/mail/smtp.go:136-142`; `cmd/linkctrl/jobs.go:202-203` runs `runMail` **before** `runWebhooks` in the one `case <-outbox.C` | Found while closing F82, which is the identical shape: the comment at `mail.go:58-61` makes the same claim F82 falsified — a backlog *"drains over several runs instead of holding the job for minutes"* — and twenty messages at the default 10s is 200 seconds spent inline on the select that reads every job ticker, whose channels hold one tick. Closing F82 removed half of that harm class and left this half: the webhook drain no longer stalls the scheduler, and the mail drain that runs immediately before it still can. Reproducing it needs a relay that blackholes rather than refuses, which is what a firewalled SMTP host looks like | **Moderate.** Not tenant-triggerable, and that is the whole of the difference from F82: the relay is one host an operator named in configuration, so this needs the operator's own SMTP to fail in the one way that is slow rather than the several that are fast. The consequence when it does is F82's exactly — automation stops firing on its clock, domain re-verification does not run, rollups go stale, and the outbox that is stalling is also the thing invitations are waiting in. The fix is the one D95 records, applied to a second `Drain`; whether the two packages should share it rather than spell it twice is the part worth deciding rather than assuming | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the moderate non-security set. Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45** (same entry). D95's fix applied to the second `Drain`: `SendConcurrency = DrainBatch`, a bounded channel, a mutex over the error slice, and a `sync.WaitGroup` waited on before `Drain` returns. **The leadership constraint was checked rather than assumed**, which is what the row's open question turned on: `runMail` calls `withLeadership` exactly as `runWebhooks` does and `ClaimDueMail` is `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` exactly as the webhook claim is, so D95's reasoning transfers whole — the advisory lock sits on a pooled connection released when the function returns, and `pg_try_advisory_lock` is session-scoped, so no goroutine may outlive the call and none does. **The one thing D95 does not cover is named rather than inherited**: a webhook batch spreads over as many receivers as it has rows, this one opens up to twenty sessions to the single relay the operator configured. A relay capping concurrency lower refuses the extra ones and those messages retry with backoff, so nothing is lost, but an outbox held continuously above that cap for `MaxAttempts` abandons the overflow — accepted deliberately and written into `internal/mail/mail.go`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/configuration.md` and CHANGELOG.md. **Spelled twice rather than shared**, which is the part the row asked to be decided: the two bounds are different decisions that agree on twenty for different reasons, and this milestone has already recorded why an abstraction built to make unrelated call sites look alike is one more thing to get wrong. Driven by `TestOneMailDrainSpendsOneSendRatherThanTwenty`, sabotage-verified. **F90 is untouched in both directions** — same batch size, same ordering, still no per-tenant term. |
| F32 | M32.9 | Invitation and email-verification tokens are stored **in clear** in `mail_outbox.body`, so a read of the database yields redeemable credentials | `internal/mail/mail.go:139` (`Body: body`, rendered at enqueue time), reached from `internal/invite/invite.go:749` and `internal/signup/signup.go:288` | Reproduced against a real database: a token pulled out of `mail_outbox.body` **by SQL alone** hashed to `invitations.token_hash` and redeemed into an **admin** membership; the body still contains the token after send, and `PurgeFinishedMail` is age-only (30 days), never scrubbing. Four narrowings the finder missed. (a) It needs `SMTP_HOST` set **and** `SIGNUP_MODE != closed`; under the shipped default the leaked token is **refused** — verified. (b) The signup half is near-harmless: verification ends at the sign-in form and the plaintext password never survived the signup request, so the attacker force-completes a registration they cannot sign in as. (c) `docs/SECURITY.md:45` is an **over-read** — it sits in the Invitations row and describes how the invitation *record* stores its token, which is true. (d) The sentence that actually convicts is `docs/SECURITY.md:241`, which already discloses that the outbox stores each message rendered — so the design is accepted; what is false is its closing clause *"the first mail this ships, the audit-growth warning, contains no secret"*, written in M26 (`13df367`) and broken by M27's `invitation.txt` (`0a5b1fd`) and M29's `verification.txt` (`24b3c89`) without revisiting the paragraph | **Moderate.** Genuinely falsifies `01200_invitations.sql:34-35` — *"A database leak hands over no redeemable invites"* — and literally falsifies `01400_signup.sql:43-44`. Real window is 7 days per unredeemed invitation (`INVITE_TTL`), 24h for verification. On a mailer-configured instance a **read-only** database compromise becomes an authenticated foothold at up to admin. Scrubbing `body` on `MarkMailSent` would collapse it to minutes, at the cost of the operator-inspectability the outbox exists for — a trade worth recording as a decision rather than making silently | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the moderate security cluster | **M45** (`docs/build-notes/decisions.md`, *M45, three places a secret was sitting still*). Scrubbed at the terminal transition: `MarkMailSent` and `MarkMailFailed` set `body = ''` in the same statement that sets the status, and `03200` erases the bodies already in the table and adds `CHECK (status = 'pending' OR body = '')` so the scrub cannot be quietly removed. Every kind, not a sensitive-kind list — a list defaults new templates to unsafe, which is exactly how (d)'s sentence rotted. The two rejected options and what each cost are recorded in full: a send-time reference needs a plaintext home for a token that exists only as a hash, and shortened retention sets a window in days against a seven-day token. **Not fully closed as a class, and said so**: a *pending* row still carries its body, so an instance whose relay is down holds a live token for as long as the invitation is redeemable. `01200`, `01400`, `01100`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/configuration.md` and `.env.example` all now say that instead of the stronger claim. Asserted by `TestInvitationDeliveryWithAndWithoutAMailer/and_the_delivered_row_keeps_no_token` and `TestAnAbandonedMessageKeepsItsRecordAndNotItsContents`, both sabotage-verified |

All four escape routes checked and closed: `slog.Any` renders errors via `Error()` (confirmed in `json_handler.go`); `redactAttrs` (`logging.go:65`) matches on the attribute **key**, which here is `"error"`; and `FEED_URL` is a plain `string`, not `config.Secret` (`config.go:318`). Fires on DNS failure, refused connection, TLS error and timeout — the common one at `FEED_TIMEOUT=2s`. One narrowing: `FEED_METHOD` defaults to **POST**, so the user's destination is only in the URL under `FEED_METHOD=GET`. The killer fact the finder missed is that the boot log already does it right — `cmd/linkctrl/main.go:461` logs `feedClient.Endpoint()`, deliberately query-stripped — and `internal/platform/postgres/postgres.go:62` refuses to wrap for exactly this reason: *"Do not wrap: the error may echo the DSN, which contains the password"* | **Moderate.** A live third-party credential reaching a lower-trust sink on an ordinary error path, precisely when logs get shipped and pasted into tickets. Replacing rather than filtering also removes the **user's destination** under `FEED_METHOD=GET`, where it is a query parameter on the request URL: the same mechanism, not a second fix. The `*url.Error` is not wrapped, only its cause is, so nothing can `errors.As` back to the URL. Asserted by `TestATransportFailureNamesTheEndpointAndNotTheCredential`, sabotage-verified — the control run reproduces this row's evidence exactly, destination included |
| F35 | M32.9 | The feed disclosure strips a credential from the query string but not from the URL's userinfo, so `https://key:secret@feed.example/` is rendered verbatim to **every signed-in user** | `internal/feed/feed.go:185-193` (`Endpoint`), rendered at `internal/ui/templates/pages/feeds.html:57` and returned by `GET /api/v1/feeds` | Reproduced: `Endpoint("https://apikey:SECRET@api.feed.example/v1/check")` returns the string unchanged, while the query-string form is stripped correctly. Both halves. `Endpoint()` now parses and rebuilds from scheme, host and path — an allowlist, so a spelling nobody thought of is excluded by default rather than needing to be added to the strip list — and config validation **refuses** `FEED_URL` carrying userinfo at boot, pointing at `FEED_AUTH_HEADER`/`FEED_AUTH_TOKEN`, which is the discipline this bypassed. A breaking change for an instance that had one, stated in CHANGELOG.md. **The residue is named rather than closed**: a credential in a path segment is indistinguishable from a path, survives this, and is still printed on `/feeds`; the path is kept because dropping it would gut the disclosure, and `docs/configuration.md`, `.env.example` and `docs/SECURITY.md` each warn about it. | F97 | M44.9 | An API key whose owner has been removed from the organization still authenticates, still carries that organization's tenancy, reaches one state-changing endpoint outside its own scopes, and can renew itself indefinitely with no administrator able to revoke it | `internal/auth/apikey.go:589` restricts the identity to the **row's** stored scopes with no membership check on the way, and `:426-451`'s `workspace_id != nil` branch checks none either, while the `else` branch that does is unreachable because `Create` always sets it; `internal/auth/apikey_rotation.go:142-197` checks only `IsAPIKey`, revoked, expired, grace and successor under lock — never membership or `users.status`; `narrowScopes` at `:320-360` copies the predecessor row's scopes; `successorExpiry` at `:394-406` gives the successor *"the predecessor's lifetime, measured from now"*; `internal/store/query/apikeys.sql:85` (`RevokeAPIKey`) keys on `user_id` | `restrictTo` does not disarm the successor, because `narrowScopes` copies the **row's** stored scopes rather than the actor's live permissions, so full scopes are persisted and go live again if the membership ever returns. Each generation can rotate again, so the chain is unbounded. Two sub-claims were **refuted** and are not part of this row: `ListAPIKeysForUser` *is* organization-scoped (`apikeys.sql:37-38`), and `RecordAPIKeyRotation` writes an organization-scoped `apikey.rotated` audit row, so an admin holding `audit.read` can *see* a rotation. What they cannot do is revoke it — there is no organization-scoped revoke statement, and the vocabulary has no `apikey.created` or `apikey.revoked`. **Folded in from F43 (M32.9), 2026-08-04**, which is the root this row extends and carries the reproduction: in a rolled-back transaction, after `DeleteMembership`, `GetAPIKeyByPrefix` still resolves the key, `GetUserPermissions` returns 0, and `resolveWorkspace` — the dead branch — would have refused. F43's own two corrections travel with it. The finder's stated contradiction was **wrong**: `apikey.go:396-399` promises only that *permissions* are recomputed, and they are, to empty; the real contradiction is the comment on the dead branch, which names this exact state and prescribes *"Answering 'invalid' is honest for both"* — applied only where it cannot be reached — together with `team_test.go:1004`, which asserts an orphaned identity must carry neither org nor workspace, on the session path. The impact bound was also wrong: sweeping every route found `POST /api/v1/organizations` **reachable and writing**, because `CreateOrganization` bypasses `orgs.create` when `CountUserMemberships` is 0 (the D36 first-organization path), carries no `requireSessionActor`, and `orgs.create` is not in `NonDelegableScopes` — so a key scoped to `links.read` alone can create an organization and own it. | **Moderate.** F43 filed the same orphaned credential at **Minor** on the strength of it being *"One-shot"*; rotation makes it repeatable and self-renewing, which is why the two could not be triaged separately. Bounded to workspace-bound keys: an organization-wide key dies correctly, because `apikey.go:568-577` answers `ErrAPIKeyInvalid` on `ErrNoWorkspace`. No tenant data is reachable in either direction and the org-creation door is a scope bypass rather than a privilege escalation, since the same human can do it with a session by design; what keeps this above cosmetic is that a tested invariant is violated on a path the test does not cover, and an administrator who removes somebody reasonably believes their credentials into the tenant are inert. Fix note: adding a membership check to `Rotate` alone leaves F43's own door — `CreateOrganization`'s zero-membership bypass — open, and neither gives an administrator a revoke path, which needs a new organization-scoped statement | Approved as work 2026-08-04 — [M45's triage](decisions.md), the moderate security cluster. **[F43](#closed) (M32.9) was merged into this row** by M45's queue hygiene, 2026-08-04: one missing membership check, and this row already said it *"strictly extends F43"* while F43's severity rested on a premise this row falsifies. F43 was not itself approved; the fix note above already required its door to be answered, so the approved work is unchanged in extent and the two limbs are now visible to whoever takes it. Fixed under **M45's own number**: m45.md's Findings bullet is this work, so no milestone is reopened. | **M45**, all three limbs the fix note required. `internal/store/query/apikeys.sql` — `GetAPIKeyByPrefix` returns `owner_is_member`, an `EXISTS` over the membership covering the key's scope with the same predicate `GetUserPermissions` uses, and `internal/auth/apikey.go:Authenticate` answers `ErrAPIKeyInvalid` on it, which is what the dead `resolveWorkspace` branch's own comment prescribed. **F43's door closes with it and needed no credential-type branch**: an authenticated key now always has a live membership, so `CountUserMemberships` is never zero for one and `CreateOrganization`'s D36 bypass is unreachable by a key — recorded at that call site so the next reader does not add a `requireSessionActor` the inherited rule forbids. `GetAPIKeyForRotation` re-reads membership and account status under its lock, so a removal racing a rotation wins. And the revoke path: `RevokeAPIKeyInOrganization`, reached from `Revoke` when the id is not the actor's own, gated on `apikeys.write` from an organization-wide membership (D44) and answering not-found otherwise so ids stay unprobeable — audited as the new `apikey.revoked`, because the vocabulary's reason for omitting revocation (*the person is the record*) stops holding once the credential's owner is not present. Driven by `TestAKeyDiesWithTheMembershipItActsThrough` and `TestAnOrganizationWideAdminRevokesSomebodyElsesKey` (`test/integration/apikey_test.go`) and `TestRotationRefusesAnIdentityWhoseMembershipWentAwayMidFlight` (`test/integration/apikey_rotation_test.go`), each sabotage-verified. |

The concurrency is **inside** `Drain` with a `sync.WaitGroup` waited on before it returns, which is what makes this row's own fix note inapplicable rather than survived: no goroutine outlives the function `withLeadership` releases the advisory lock on, so D77 holds unchanged. [F90](#open) and [F91](#closed) were out of scope and stay open: F90 is untouched in both directions, and F91 is no longer reachable at any permitted `WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT` without being fixed. **Superseded in one detail, 2026-08-04 (M45):** [F91](#closed) no longer stays open — M45's queue hygiene re-derived it against this fix and closed it, since its whole mechanism was the twenty sequential dials this commit removed. | F77 | M44.9 | A host spelled with Unicode label separators or fullwidth characters walks past every destination tier, and is stored and served | `internal/link/destination.go:147` is the whole of host canonicalisation (`strings.TrimRight(strings.ToLower(u.Hostname()), ".")`); `looksNumeric` at `:219` uses `strings.LastIndexByte(host, '.')`; `internal/link/punycode.go:37` returns early unless a label starts `xn--` | **Reproduced live against the current build.** `POST /api/v1/links` accepted and stored `http://169。254。169。254/latest/meta-data/`, `http://１６９.２５４.１６９.２５４/`, `http://ｌｏｃａｌｈｏｓｔ/`, `http://metadata。google。internal/computeMetadata/v1/` and `http://127。0。0。1/admin`, each percent-escaped into storage, while the control `http://169.254.169.254/` was refused 422. No `destination.blocked` row is written for the accepted ones. There is no IDNA/UTS-46 step anywhere and `golang.org/x/net` is deliberately absent from go.mod (decisions.md ~5847). A browser's WHATWG host parser percent-decodes the host, then runs UTS-46 ToASCII, which maps U+3002/U+FF0E/U+FF61 to `.` and the fullwidth ranges to ASCII. **The finder's belief that Go's client is immune was refuted by measurement**: `net/http`'s `canonicalAddr` calls the standard library's vendored `golang.org/x/net/idna`, and a dial probe showed the stored form connecting to `169.254.169.254:80` | **High**, the tier F26 was given, over a wider spelling space. Not critical: nothing reads the response back, and the webhook dialer's `guardDial` judges the *resolved address*, so the private-address line holds for the one server-initiated fetch. But `guardDial` judges addresses only, so the Postgres blocklist, the shortener list and the homograph tier — all name-based — are bypassed, and this server can be made to POST signed workspace payloads to a host the operator blocked. Falsifies `docs/SECURITY.md:40` (*"a name cannot be spelled past a check that would otherwise refuse it"*) and m30.md's central claim, so it **reopens M30** rather than F26, whose `TrimRight` fix is correct for what it names. Fix note: canonicalise, do not reject — refusing non-ASCII kills `müller.de`, which a recorded decision protects — and a hand-rolled map of the three separators misses the fullwidth spellings, which carry no separator at all. Only full UTS-46 catches both, the ToASCII form must be what is **stored**, and it needs `golang.org/x/net/idna` in go.mod, which the punycode decision refused. **That dependency is a decision for the owner, not a correction** | No | **M30, reopened a second time.** `canonicalHost` in `internal/link/destination.go` puts a non-ASCII host through UTS-46 ToASCII before any tier looks, at the single fold D46 already established, with the trailing-dot trim moved *after* the mapping so a separator that is not yet a dot still becomes one. The stored value is the ToASCII form. The profile is WHATWG's `domain to ASCII` and deliberately not `idna.Lookup`, which refuses `my_host.example`, `under_score.example.com` and `r3---sn-apo3qvuoxuxbt-j5pe.googlevideo.com`; an all-ASCII host skips the mapping as `net/http`'s own `idnaASCII` does; a host UTS-46 cannot map is refused with the untiered `invalid` code. `LINKCTRL_DESTINATION_BLOCKLIST` and `blocked_hosts.txt` fold through the same function, or the fix would have re-broken the sentence it was closing. D93. `TestAUnicodeSpellingDoesNotDefeatAnyTier` pairs each of the five spellings with its ASCII control and pins the profile; `TestAUnicodeHostIsRefusedAndRecorded` proves the audit row that was missing; both fail on a build without the fold. |

The resulting session is not a key, so `requireSessionActor` no longer refuses. | F26 | M32.9 | A trailing dot on the hostname defeats the unappealable tier **and** the embedded high-confidence list — `http://169.254.169.254./`, `http://127.0.0.1./`, `http://localhost./` and `http://metadata.google.internal./` are all accepted and stored | `internal/link/destination.go:198` (`looksNumeric` returns false on the empty last label), `:169` (the `localhost` equality test), and `internal/link/blocking.go:218` (a bare `embeddedHosts[host]` lookup) | Reproduced live on :8081 through the real handler: `POST /api/v1/links` answered **201** for `http://169.254.169.254./latest/meta-data/` and **422 `unappealable.private_address`** for the dotless control; `PATCH` is affected identically; the stored value is 302'd verbatim at serve time and **no `destination.blocked` audit row is written** for any dotted attempt. Confirmed independently against HEAD's source in a scratch tree, and the image was checked not stale for `internal/link/`. A second verifier, working the design angle, established the correction that matters: **LinkCtrl never fetches a destination server-side** — the only two `http.Client`s in the tree are the opt-in feed and the container healthcheck — so this is a stored **open redirect**, not SSRF, and the attacker cannot read the response. That verifier widened the affected spellings to nine and validated a one-line fix (`strings.TrimSuffix` on the folded host at `destination.go:130`) that closes all three tiers with `go test ./internal/link/` green and no test edits — including `destination_test.go:133`, which requires `https://example.com./` to stay accepted. Not critical: no server-side fetch, so the victim's own browser makes the request and the attacker learns nothing. `localhost.` needs no WHATWG argument — glibc resolves it, and `curl http://localhost.:8081/healthz` returned 200 | Yes — owner approved 2026-08-01 on M32.9's triage; **reopens [M30](phase-details/m30.md)**. Closed by folding the trailing dot away in `ValidateDestination` — one place, before the address, numeric and localhost checks — so that every tier reads a host that has already been canonicalized (D46). Canonicalized rather than refused, as this row's *Reviewed* cell required: `https://example.com./path` is accepted and **stored** as `https://example.com/path`, so the value a visitor is later handed is the value the tiers judged. This row's evidence is right on every point and is left as written, including its four `Where` sites. One correction, and it is the reason the fix is not the one-liner the row validated: `strings.TrimSuffix` trims a single dot, and `http://127.0.0.1../` has an empty last label after one trim just as `http://127.0.0.1./` does before any — measured, by running the row's proposed fix against the new test, which accepted it. `strings.TrimRight` is what closes the spelling as well as the character. `looksNumeric`'s empty-label branch, which this row correctly names as the mechanism at `destination.go:198`, now answers true rather than false; nothing reaches it once the fold is in, so it changes no behaviour and exists so the helper fails closed if a third caller appears. The test gap the row exposes is closed too: `TestUnappealableTierHasNoOverrideSwitch` guarded a promise about what may be *accepted* with a reflection walk over `DestinationPolicy`'s *fields*, so it never tried a host. `TestATrailingDotDoesNotDefeatAnyTier` (`internal/link/blocking_test.go`) feeds each dotted spelling against its dotless control through all four mechanisms and the embedded list, and `TestATrailingDotIsRefusedAndRecorded` (`test/integration/blocking_test.go`) holds the consequence this row found and the unit tests cannot see — the refusal now writes its `destination.blocked` row, on create and on update, where before there was no refusal and therefore no record. Both were shown red against the shipped behaviour first. |

| F27 | M32.9 | M28's workspace-scoped memberships confer **organization-wide** authority: a workspace-scoped admin can grant themselves into any workspace, re-role and remove org-wide members, issue org-wide invitations, and — with no `Grant` at all — promote their own org-wide row to admin. Three calls later, after `Grant(self, W2, admin)` succeeded, the same guard passes and she renames W2. It cannot be fixed at `Grant` alone — the self-promotion route uses no `Grant` at all. | M28, 2026-08-02. Closed by D44: a write is authorized by the membership whose **scope covers its target**, not by the identity's union. `auth.MembershipAuthority` (`internal/auth/authority.go`) over a new `ListMembershipAuthority` query answers, per scope, whether the actor holds the permission there and at which rank; `In(nil)` is the organization-wide scope, which only an organization-wide membership reaches. `manageable` — shared by `ChangeRole` and `Remove` — resolves it against the target membership's own scope before any rank is compared, and `resolveRole`'s ceiling now reads that rank rather than `Identity.RoleRank`, which closes the second route this row's evidence did not reach: an organization-wide **admin** who also held a workspace-scoped **owner** row would otherwise have carried rank 10 against organization-wide targets. `Grant` resolves the authority in the workspace being granted into, which is the same question `canInWorkspace` already asked for `workspace.write`. `invite.Create` and `DeleteOrganization` require the organization-wide scope, because what they produce and destroy is organization-wide. `refuseLastOwner` became `guardOwnerSet`, called on promotion as well as demotion so every change to the owner set takes the same lock — its promotion refusal is deliberate depth rather than the operative guard, since after D44 minting an organization-wide owner structurally requires being one; the decisions entry says so plainly. `members.html`'s workspace select is filtered where the page loads, and `Members` computes `Manageable` per row against the same authority. Five integration tests drive a workspace-scoped identity as the **actor** of a write — the gap this row named as the reason the suite was green — and all five were shown red first by neutralizing the scope filter, which reproduced the escalation including the rows it wrote.
